# Ponor — Design Plan

> **v1 scope · rev 6 · 2026-11-19** — v1 is the **v1** GitHub milestone, and #22 says why each issue is in it (§9)
> Core decisions: **Supabase** backend · **offline-first** · **optional sign-in** · photos in **v1.1**
> Name and visual identity locked in §0; both themes ship from M0.
> Styled copies of this document and of the visual identity live as Claude artifacts; their links are kept out of this public repo.

A free, offline-first dive log for iPhone, Android, and tablets — web to follow — that *fills itself in*: after the first dive of a trip, you only touch what changed.

---

## 0. Name & visual identity

**The app is called Ponor.** In Czech, *ponor* is the submersion itself — the draft of a vessel, how deep a thing sits; the root of *ponořit se*, to submerge. In English it is a karst term: the opening where a surface stream leaves the daylight and becomes a subterranean river. The same meaning in both languages, the same pronunciation (PO-nor), it declines cleanly in Czech, and it spells itself when heard. **`ponor.app` and `ponor.online` are registered.**

Checked and rejected on availability: *Slate*, *Bezel*, *Sextant* (crowded on the App Store), *Halocline* (Halocline GmbH holds software-class trademarks), *Atoll* (Forsk's registered software mark), *Manta* (an existing paper dive logbook), *Deco* (TP-Link, plus an existing dive-planning app). Note that every competitor is a dive+log mashup — Dive Log, Diving Log, Divog, Diviac, DiveMate, Diveboard, Depth, Subsurface — so a name in a diver's own vocabulary stands apart by default. Still outstanding: a direct App Store and Play Store search, and a trademark register check.

### 0.1 Colour is depth, and colour is nothing else

Water strips colour out in a fixed order — red is gone by about 5 m, then orange, then yellow, then green; blue is what is left. The depth scale **is** that sequence, so a depth always carries a colour and the colour is physically true.

| Band | Dark | Light |
|---|---|---|
| 0–6 m | `#FF6B4A` | `#E04A28` |
| 6–12 m | `#FF9F43` | `#C2600A` |
| 12–20 m | `#F5CE3E` | `#8F7000` |
| 20–30 m | `#3FCB94` | `#0E9F6E` |
| 30–40 m | `#2E9BE0` | `#0B76B8` |
| 40 m + | `#6673E4` | `#3A49C0` |

Because colour is spoken for, **every control is monochrome** — the primary button is simply inverted ink. Depth is always redundantly encoded by the number itself, so the scale never carries meaning on its own: colour-blind safe, and legible in glare.

### 0.2 Theme tokens

Both themes ship from M0. The app follows the OS and the choice is overridable in settings; components read whichever token set `resolveScheme` returns, so the same component serves both.

| Token | Role | Dark | Light |
|---|---|---|---|
| `bg` | App ground | `#080B0F` | `#EDEEEA` |
| `surface` | Cards, fields, charts | `#111820` | `#FFFFFF` |
| `border` | Hairlines, dividers | `#212D38` | `#CDD3CC` |
| `fg` | Text and values | `#F0F5F8` | `#0D1216` |
| `fg-muted` | Labels, units, metadata | `#7C8D9A` | `#5A6670` |
| `action` / `action-fg` | Primary button — inverted ink | `#F0F5F8` / `#080B0F` | `#0D1216` / `#EDEEEA` |
| `sheet` | A sheet's own ground (issue #3) | `#0F161D` | `#F4F5F1` |
| `sheet-item` | An action card on a sheet | `#111820` | `#E4E6E0` |
| `scrim` | Dims the screen a sheet rises over | `rgba(8,11,15,.66)` | `rgba(13,18,22,.42)` |
| `depth-1…6` | Depth scale — **data only, never chrome** | see §0.1 | see §0.1 |

**Type:** *Archivo* for UI and display, *IBM Plex Mono* for all data — depths, pressures, durations, timestamps — with tabular figures wherever digits align in a column. Both carry Latin Extended, which Czech needs (ě š č ř ž ů). The wordmark is Archivo, uppercase, tracked +0.2 em, following the vernacular of dive-instrument brands.

### 0.3 The mark

A dive profile under a wavy surface: a steep descent, bottom time, then a staged ascent that ends in open water rather than climbing back to the waterline. Both of those are load-bearing, and it took four rounds of rendering to find out why. A profile that closes at the top against a straight surface line reads unmistakably as a **bucket** — two matching diagonal walls over a flat base. Ending the ascent in open space removes the second wall; making the surface a wave says "water" instantly. Stroked in the depth gradient, flat single-colour below ~32 px, and the same shape the app will draw for an imported dive — so the icon is a sample of the product rather than a badge stuck on it.

The gradient stops are read from `depthScale.dark` at build time, so the mark cannot drift from the palette. The wave itself is stroked `#5A6C78` — deliberately **not** a theme token. It is a brand-asset colour belonging to the mark alone, chosen to sit quietly behind the profile on the icon's dark ground; it is never used in the interface, where `fg-muted` serves that role.

### 0.4 Profiles are drawn only from real data

A dive row shows the **coloured depth number and no graphic**. When a dive has a real sample series — from a dive-computer import (M3o) — that same row grows a profile sparkline and the dive detail gains a profile chart, automatically and without a redesign. The app never draws a schematic curve interpolated from max depth, average depth and duration: an invented shape on a dive log reads as recorded data, and it isn't.

That claim was checked rather than assumed (design pass after M1b): the chart occupies the space between the detail hero and the first cluster, which is empty today, and nothing above it moves. **The curve is stroked through the depth gradient** — a profile is depth against time and colour already encodes depth, so the chart's colour is data under the same rule as everything else, and the chart becomes the same object as the mark in §0.3. In a row the sparkline stays **monochrome**: the number already carries the colour there, and at 46 px a gradient reads as a smudge, so the sparkline contributes shape and the number magnitude. A row without samples shows no sparkline and no placeholder, exactly as a dive without a depth shows no number.

**Built 2026-09-10** (M3p), and the design above needed no amendment to accommodate it — only its *post-v1* clause, which import had already overtaken. The card landed in the space M1b measured for it, the row grew its shape, and nothing above either moved. `DepthProfile` and `ProfileSparkline` (components/) draw from `dive_samples` and from nothing else: a null series, an empty one, a single point, a series whose depths carry no band, and a series with no elapsed time between its first point and its last all render **nothing at all** — no card, no axis, no placeholder, and no reserved row where one would have gone. The geometry is pure and lives in `domain/profile.ts`, so every judgement the drawing rests on is falsifiable without rendering anything.

**The caption states the interval the device actually recorded, not the one its file declares.** `medianIntervalS` takes the median delta between samples, because the reference dive's export declares 10 s while its real deltas run 9.74–10.10. Printing the header would be quoting a claim back at the diver, and the difference between a claim and a measurement is this section's whole subject.

**A consumption companion joined the depth curve** (owner's request): instantaneous RMV in l/min over a **60-second moving window** (`PROFILE_RMV_WINDOW_S`, domain/consumption.ts), drawn in muted ink and scaled to its own ceiling, because §0.1 spends colour on depth and has none left to give a second series. **That ceiling is deliberately never labelled** (owner's call, 2026-09-10, on seeing the card on a phone): it sat in a column right of the plot, and a column that sizes to its own text decides the chart's width — the same dive drew narrower in Czech imperial (`1,41 cu ft/min`) than in English metric (`40.0 l/min`), and a dive with no gas drew wider than the dive above it. The scrub readout states the gas at the finger exactly, wherever the finger is, so the ceiling only ever said how tall the tallest point was — and it was charging the drawing for the privilege. §0.4's own instinct governs it three times, and each rule costs visible chart to remove an artefact by construction rather than by tuning. **A point exists only where a complete window does** — a window that shrinks as the series runs out is a reading taken over less time than it claims, and the first implementation's shrinking window put a phantom 36 l/min spike at the dive's end, the tallest point on the chart and the least meaningful. **No point where the window's mean depth is under 1.5 m** (`PROFILE_RMV_MIN_DEPTH_M`): approaching the surface `ata` approaches 1, so any pressure change inflates, and a diver up there is often off the regulator entirely. **And a gap stays a gap** — where consecutive points sit more than two windows apart the trace breaks rather than bridging an interval nobody measured, and the scrub readout shows an em dash across it for the same reason.

**Both figures come out of one arithmetic.** Every step routes through `derived.ts`'s `rmvFrom`, which already owned the whole-dive figure the Stats screen shows — so this curve and that number cannot tell a diver two different things about one dive. A second copy of the formula here would have been free to drift, and the fact that the two agree would then have been luck rather than structure.

### 0.5 Constraints the design must answer

- **Sunlight.** Dives are logged on an open deck at noon. The light theme exists for this; contrast is a functional requirement, not a taste question.
- **Wet hands, one thumb.** Tap targets never below 48 dp, and the primary action sits in the bottom third of the screen. **Two recorded exceptions.** The first is **a map annotation** (M3c). An annotation is hit-tested over the mark it actually **draws**, so a transparent 48 dp box around a 14 pt dot buys nothing at all — the dot was simply unpressable, and the badge beside it worked only because it was bigger. The floor is met by making the mark itself 26 pt, which is under 48 and is the largest a map mark can be without covering what it points at.

  The second is **the dive detail's site and centre rows**, which M3c made navigate and never gave a target — roughly 21 pt inside a 10 pt cluster rhythm. **Both fixes cost something a stylesheet cannot weigh**: a 48 pt floor more than doubles two rows of a dense column that exists to be read at a glance, and slop deep enough to reach the floor **overruns the gap to the row directly beneath**, handing presses aimed at the site to the centre — which is the failure this project rates worst of all. Recorded as an exemption rather than settled, on the grounds that both destinations are also reachable through the directories' own full-size rows. **It wants deciding on a device**, which is where every other decision of this shape has been settled.
- **Czech runs 20–30 % longer** than English. Labels wrap to two lines rather than truncate.

### 0.6 Screen composition

Designed after M1b, once the built screens showed the tokens being *obeyed* without being *used* — every element the same size, and the depth colour reduced to a small number at the end of a row. The rule below is what makes the palette legible.

**Search and `+` share one capsule at the top right.** It began at the bottom — measured off iOS 26 Messages, and correct while Ponor had no tab bar — and moved when the tabs arrived and claimed that strip (§3). The capsule follows Calendar: one rounded glass container, magnifier and `+` inside it as **equal monochrome glyphs**, no border and no rule. Liquid Glass where the device has it (`expo-glass-effect`, guarded by `isLiquidGlassAvailable()`), a plain `surface` capsule everywhere else — which is the common case and must look deliberate rather than degraded. The `+` gets **no extra emphasis** over the magnifier: §10 already rules out a hue, and size or weight would reintroduce the "is this data or chrome" question §0.1 exists to prevent.

**The tab bar is iOS's own, drawn with Ponor's glyphs** (issue #2, the owner's call on 2026-11-16, after clicking through the Claude design "Ponor tab bar", which showed three options on real simulator screens). The bar itself — a floating Liquid Glass capsule, its size, material, inset and shrink-on-scroll, and the grey glass bubble behind the selected tab — is `NativeTabs`'s `UITabBarController`, as §3 decided, and the app sets only three things on it: **ink** (`fg-muted` at rest, `fg` selected, unchanged on iOS), the labels' **face** (Archivo, as every piece of chrome) and **glyphs**, which are the canvas's set:

- **Dives is Ponor's mark** — §0.3's profile under its wave, redrawn for 22 units as the canvas draws it (one stop on the ascent, a shorter wave; `assets/tab-mark.svg`) and **one ink**, since the gradient is colour and §0.1 has spent colour on depth. It is a **vector in the app itself**, which the bar tints exactly as it tints a symbol: `plugins/withTabGlyphs.js` writes the SVG into the iOS asset catalog (kept as a vector) and into an Android vector drawable at prebuild, and the tab asks for it by name (`tab_mark`). On iOS it is tinted because the bar has icon colours — `iconColor` at rest, `tintColor` selected; expo-router then renders the image as a template, overriding the catalog's own flag — so `iconColor` on iOS is load-bearing, not just a grey. The browser's bar, which cannot read either, draws PNGs built from the same SVG. **28 pt, measured**, and stated once, as the SVG's own size: in iOS 26's bar the SF Symbols beside it draw about 23–24 pt wide, and at 28 the mark's ink is the same width, so the four read as one set. Unlike the first-run mark its wave keeps full ink: the canvas subordinates it by width (1.6 against 2) at this size, and half of a muted ink would lose it on the glass.
- **Settings is the canvas's two sliders** (`assets/tab-sliders.svg`, `tab_sliders`), Ponor's drawing and a vector by the same route as the mark — the owner's call over iOS's own three-slider symbol.
- **Map** keeps the folded map (`map`) and **Stats** is filled bars (`chart.bar.fill`; Material's `bar_chart` already was).

**The two vectors are native configuration.** They exist only in a binary built after the plugin ran; a dev client or TestFlight build from before it draws Dives and Settings with **no glyph**, because the bar asks for an image by name and gets nothing back. Merging this change therefore means a new native build (EAS) for every device that runs the app.

**Where the bar departs from the canvas, deliberately:** the canvas's selected tab is an inverted-ink pill (72 × 48, radius 24) and iOS draws its own bubble, because the native bar takes no selection colour on iOS and the pill would need a bar drawn in JavaScript, which is what §3 rejected and what was prototyped and turned down again here; the capsule's metrics and material (64 · r32 · inset 16 / 24, `surface` at 72 % + blur) are iOS's, so the canvas's *new values to tokenise* for the bar have nothing to tokenise — the app does not draw it; and Map is the folded map the canvas *draws*, though its note 11 says "a pin". **Android does draw the canvas's pill**: Material puts its indicator behind the selected icon, and it is `action` with the glyph on it in `action-fg` (the word under it stays `fg`) — the owner's call, made without an Android screen to look at, so it is unchecked until one is. The browser's JS bar draws the same glyphs through `TabBarGlyph`.

**The magnifier is a trigger, not a field.** Tapping it opens a full-screen search whose input is docked at the **bottom, on the keyboard**, with the list cleared to results and a visible way out — measured off Messages, which anchors its field at the bottom rather than moving it to the top when search is entered. A field under the header would put the input as far from the thumb and the keys as the screen allows; that arrangement was rejected once already for being the brightest object in the app, and again for this. **The site and centre picker is the one search that puts its field at the top**, deliberately and on the owner's call (issue #19) — it is described with the form below.

**The Dives screen is a native large-title screen, and the capsule floats beside the title.** Measured off iOS 26 rather than recalled, and it took four shapes to get here. The large **"Dives"** title sits at the same height as every other screen's title — the safe-area inset plus the ordinary heading padding, through the one `screenTopInset` every screen root asks the device for (§4.1) — and the search-and-`+` capsule **floats to its trailing side, over the scroll content**, rather than occupying a bar of its own above it. The shared `screen` style deliberately carries no `paddingTop`, so a root that forgets to compose the inset fails visibly rather than quietly rendering slightly too high. **No compact title fades in when the title scrolls away**: the tab bar already shows which screen is selected, so the screen is never unidentified, and a scroll-driven crossfade is machinery with an edge case at every rubber-band.

**The title says what the logbook adds up to** (M1l, the owner's design sheet). Under "Dives", in muted mono: `128 dives · 96 h 12 min · deepest 41.2 m`. Four things about it are rules rather than styling. **The three figures are §3's Stats tab**, word for word, so they have one owner (`domain/logbookStats.ts`, §4.1) and M3 renders them rather than recomputing them. **Planned dives are excluded**, as §2.4 already requires of anything called stats — they have no duration and usually no depth, and would drag every figure. **All three describe the same population: the logbook, never `dives_before`** — a diver with 100 pre-Ponor dives has a top row reading `#128` above a list of 28, and a header saying `128 dives · 3 h` would be summarising two different populations in one line; §2.5 owns the numbering, and this line summarises *the list beneath it*. **The depth takes no band colour**, though it is a depth and §0.1 says colour encodes depth: this figure is an aggregate over many dives, so any one band would be a claim about a set that no band is true of. A figure with nothing behind it is **omitted, not drawn as an em dash** — the dash belongs to a labelled row that is drawn whether or not it holds a value, and this line has no labels.

**And the line is why the header column stops short of the capsule** (M1l, found by looking, not by a test; corrected M1m). The summary runs toward the floating capsule and reaches it: at eight dives it stops 10 pt short of the glass, and at the sheet's own three-digit example it renders *behind* it. **The header's text column is therefore capped at the capsule's leading edge**, and the summary wraps to a second line under it — which is what the owner's sheet draws, `128 dives · 96 h 12 min ·` above `deepest 41.2 m`, and the wrap lands exactly where the capsule begins.

  **Capping the width alone is not enough, because a fold goes wherever width runs out.** Capped and left to wrap naturally the line broke as `8 dives · 5 h 19 min · deepest 41.2` above a second line holding nothing but `m` — every space is a break opportunity, so the figures tear before the separators do. Each figure's own spaces are therefore unbreakable and **the middots are the only folds**, which is what makes the sheet's break reproducible rather than a coincidence of one dive count. It also means `0 dives` can never break at all.

  M1l reserved the capsule's **height** instead, pushing the summary down the page, and rejected the width with arithmetic: 257 pt of remaining room for a line already 255 pt long. **That arithmetic was right and answered the wrong question** — it assumed the line had to fit on one, and the design never asked it to. The owner caught it the way he catches all of these, by holding the sheet beside the screen: the gap under the title was wrong, and the reserved height was what made it wrong. A constraint that buys its clearance out of *vertical* space, on a screen whose title is the thing being measured, will always show up as the title looking wrong.

**The app has one content column** (M1l, owner's call: *"there is no reason to have them different"*). Every screen's title, rows, hairlines and cluster labels hang off one inset — `CONTENT_INSET`, one constant read by the list, the form, the dive detail and Settings. There were two, 16 and 20, and the 20 was the form's: it had a real job once, holding room for the old `carried ×` chip's outward tap slop, and **M1h retired that job without retiring the number**. What was left justified 20 by pointing at the detail screen's 20. The owner found the survivor the way these are always found — by putting two screens side by side and seeing the titles disagree — and the same screen had already recorded the same complaint in M1d, when the detail back control sat 4 px inboard of the title directly beneath it. One column, named for the rule rather than for the form that used to own it.

**The capsule floats, and the sticky trip header is what gave way.** The owner's argument is that at rest a floating capsule covers nothing — it sits beside the title, in the space a title leaves empty — and a row passing under it while scrolling is recoverable, because you scroll on. That is right about rows and **wrong about a sticky header, which parks under the capsule for a whole trip's scroll extent instead of passing beneath it**; that is exactly how a trip's trailing date range came to be observed clipped to `…16`, twice. So **trip headers scroll like everything else** (§10). The shapes before this were a pinned bar holding title and capsule together — which cost the title the height every other screen's title has, and is what this replaces — and before that a capsule that receded on scroll, which worked and hid the `+` precisely when a diver deep in a long logbook wanted it. `useHideOnScroll` existed only for that and went with it.

**A field's label clears its rule by the same margin whether its value trails or stacks.** Found on Settings, where `Units` sat visibly tighter under its hairline than `Dives before Ponor` did. The cause was not what it looked like: the 48 dp tap floor sat on the input, and a label was positioned by centring within it — so a field whose value *trails* got the floor's leftover as clearance, while one whose value *stacks* (chips, notes, an open picker) already exceeded 48 dp, the centring became a no-op, and the row collapsed to the label's own height. The floor belongs on the row, not the input. The same correction reaches the form's own `Entry`, `Salinity` and `Water body` labels.

**Depth is the anchor of a dive row.** It is the value that actually differs dive to dive — a trip is four rows all saying "Blue Hole" — and it is the one the palette exists to encode. It is set in IBM Plex Mono Medium at 20 px (34 px on dive detail), in its band colour, right-aligned with tabular figures so a column of dives has a column of aligned decimals. Nothing else in a row gains colour.

| Element | Face | Size | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|
| Depth value | Plex Mono Medium | 20 / 34 | Band colour, tabular, right-aligned |
| Site name | Archivo Medium | 16 | Wraps to two lines, never truncates |
| Trip header | Archivo SemiBold | 11.5 | Uppercase, +0.13 em, muted; date range in mono, trailing |
| Dive number | Plex Mono | 11 | Muted, above the site name — a label, not a headline |
| Row metadata | Plex Mono | 11.5 | Time · duration · rating, middot-separated |
| Cluster label | Plex Mono | 10.5 | Uppercase, +0.14 em, muted |
| Computed value | Plex Mono | 13.5 | Muted ink, `=` prefix in a fixed-width slot before the value |

**A file has no ink, and the mark cannot follow it there** (M3i). §0.6 marks a derived value with `=` on screen; in a CSV that is **a formula** — Excel and Sheets evaluate any cell beginning `=`. The export therefore carries the mark in the **column name** (`computed_dive_number`) and includes exactly one derived figure: the dive number, which is a *name* rather than a measurement and the only one a spreadsheet cannot recompute, since it needs `dives_before` and §2.5's tie-break tiers. Surface interval, used pressure, gas used, RMV, MOD and nitrogen are all one formula away from columns already in the grid, so they stay out.

**Computed values are marked as computed.** Every value the app derives rather than the diver enters — time out, surface interval, used pressure, gas used, RMV and MOD — is prefixed with a muted `=` and sits in muted ink. The mark is an equals sign because that is literally what the value is: the result of a calculation, not something anyone typed. An earlier version used a small outlined square, which read as a rendering artefact rather than a deliberate mark — a symbol that needs a legend has already failed. A diver should never have to wonder which numbers came off their computer and which the app worked out. The rule is *derived or entered*, with no exception for arithmetic simple enough to do in your head: used pressure is start minus end, and it is marked for the same reason RMV is. Anything in `src/domain/derived.ts` is marked.

**Carry-over marks what it inherited, and says when you threw it away** (rewritten M1h, the owner's design). A prefilled field carries a **drawn return mark and a clear control, grouped together at the row's trailing edge** — the ring a glyph in a real 48 dp box, not a small mark with slop bolted on. **The mark sits with the ring rather than beside the value, and that is the whole rule**: led by the value it would trail the *label* instead, landing at a different x on every row, and it was built that way first and measured ragged — `↵` at 65 pt on one row and 84 pt on the next. Grouped at the trailing edge it is one column at 326 pt across text rows and chip rows alike, which is what the chip rows already did. One placement for the whole form rather than two. Accepting costs nothing: no confirmation, no tap. Overwriting is just typing, and drops the mark. The clear control empties the field to a real blank, **never a zero** (§10), and the row then reads `— cleared` in muted mono.

  **That third state is the point.** Before this a cleared field and a never-carried field were the same blank row, so a diver who deliberately threw away a carried buddy could not tell that from the form simply not having one — the app knew a fact it did not show. Chip groups carry one clear for the whole group, since a chip row has no per-value trailing edge to hang one on. The header says what was inherited and from which dive.

  **A value the site supplied carries no mark, and that is the mark's legend being protected rather than a gap** (M2s). The return mark's whole sentence answers *carried from which dive*, and one symbol over two sources stops it answering that — a regression for the common case in exchange for a new one. A second symbol is new vocabulary for a single case. The precedent was already here: `applyPreset` fills an entire cylinder block from a stored record on one tap and marks none of it. What the row does say is subtler and was found by looking: **the mark comes off where the site *replaced* a carried value and stays where the site merely *agrees* with it.** Without that distinction, picking a site would silently strip three marks while changing nothing — because a site created from a dive is seeded from the very dive that then reads it back.

  **Two things this paragraph used to claim and should not have.** It described the chip treatment as `carried ×`, which M1h replaced. And it said *"the form header's 'from #6' is tappable and starts the dive blank"* — **that control has never existed**, in any milestone; the only occurrences of the phrase in the source are comments quoting this sentence. It is not built and is not planned here; the header line is a caption, and if a start-blank affordance is ever wanted it is a new decision rather than the completion of an old one. Recorded rather than quietly deleted, because a spec asserting an unbuilt feature is the same drift §10 caught twice this milestone, and this one survived four.

**Marks on a map are monochrome, and this is §0.1 agreeing with itself rather than an exception to it** (M2n, and it overturned my own guess). A pin looks like the one place colour and cartography agree — and §3's unit on that screen is the **site**, badged with a count, so the ordinary mark stands for several dives at several depths. §10 has already ruled that an aggregate depth takes no band (M1l's summary line: no single band is true of a set), and colouring only the sites that happen to hold one dive would make hue mean *how many dives are here*. The second argument is the palette's own guarantee: **every colour in Ponor sits beside its own figure**, and a mark at map scale has nowhere to put one — a coloured pin would be the first place in the app where hue carries meaning alone, on the screen most likely to be read in sunlight on a deck.

  **Measured, after building it in band colours and looking**: the depth scale is tuned as **ink**, so used as a **fill** it inverts the contrast it was designed for — in light the near-black count is unreadable on the three deepest bands, in dark the white count washes out on two others. A green pin over water reads as an island, beside Apple's own green islands; a blue disc carrying a number is already Apple's road shield, and one was in the same crop. And a coloured fill leaves **no ink to say "selected"** — found by accident when a selected pin's count went dark. Rejected and named so it is not re-proposed: colouring a site only when all its dives share a band makes the *absence* of colour mean "mixed", and a site would silently lose its colour the day someone went two metres deeper. The palette still lives on that screen, where it always does — on the dive rows in the site's own sheet.

**Chrome the type scale does not cover.** Hairline separators on `border` divide dive rows, **set on each row's top edge, not its bottom**. That is not interchangeable: a top edge puts a line under the trip header, where the design wants one, while a bottom edge puts every line one position later and leaves the header touching its first row. The last row of a group therefore carries no line beneath it — groups are separated by the next header's own top padding, not by a rule. Without any of this the list is one undifferentiated column. The day strip's action is a bordered pill in tracked uppercase, not plain text, so it reads as a control rather than a label. The dive-detail back control is mono, muted and small — it is a way out, not a heading. Rating marks are **drawn**, not typed: `●` and `○` are different sizes in almost every typeface, so a rating rendered from glyphs looks broken; draw both as circles of one diameter, filled or outlined. **Leaving a screen has one treatment everywhere** — the dive-detail back control's mono/muted/small form is also the form's way out (`‹ Cancel`), so "this takes you back" reads the same wherever it appears and never competes with the primary action beneath it. A form with no visible way out was shipped once and only found by using the app: swipe-back worked, so every test passed and nothing on screen said you could leave. **"Up next" is a section header but not a trip,** so it takes full `fg` where a trip's title is muted, and puts its dive count in the same trailing slot, same mono face, that a trip fills with its date range — an empty slot there read as a date range that had failed to load. Ink versus muted ink is the only lever: §0.1 rules out a hue, and a new shape would be new vocabulary for one header. **The dive-detail back control aligns to the hero's own 16,** and the hero has no top padding at all (it was 4 until issue #3; the canvas draws `0 / 16 / 18`): the control keeps its 48 dp tap-target floor (§0.5), and the floor's leftover slack is the whole of the gap between the way out and the title.

**A pill in a left-aligned card is left-aligned** (M2f, and found again on the map and the site page). A control that centres itself inside a card whose every other line starts at the content inset reads as a second layout rather than as a control — fixed three times now, each time by someone looking at it and never by a test. **Three occurrences is a rule:** a `Pressable` given a label and no alignment inherits centring from nothing in particular, and this codebase's cards are left-aligned.

**A catalogue row goes to its page; your own dives open a sheet** (M3f, settling a rule M3e wrote when a site had no page). The map draws three kinds of mark and two of them are rows in a catalogue — those navigate, because a peek under the map would be the same facts in two places (§4.1). The third is a place the diver has dived, which is a *group of their own dives* rather than a row, so it opens a sheet; **the numeral is what says which**. The community site's sheet held a strict subset of what its page now shows, so it went rather than being kept in step.

**A control that is about the primary action is drawn with it, not at the end of the content** (M2f). §0.6 already places a *deliberate act* — *Delete preset*, *Save as preset*, and *Delete dive* until issue #3 moved it behind the detail's ⋯ — at the end of a screen's content, where it takes a reach. **That position is for an act on the page's object.** A control that changes what the *button* does belongs in the button's own block: the account screen's sign-in/sign-up toggle shipped at the end of the content and read as orphaned — 150 pt below the last row with 400 pt of empty screen beneath it, and centred while every other element was left-aligned on the content inset, which is a second idiom for one control. Centring is coherent under a full-width button and was not coherent alone in the page body. The one destructive control on that same screen stays at the end of the content, and that is the distinction rather than an exception to it: **sign-out acts on the account, the toggle acts on the button.** Since issue #24 both are the outlined button rather than muted labels — the owner could not tell them from text — and the placement rule is unchanged.

**Hand-ordering lives on a day strip, not a row.** A trip spans several days but only one may be reorderable, so a control on the trip header would be ambiguous. Days that qualify get a strip — `18 Aug · 2 dives, no times` — which also states *why* they qualify; without it a diver who adds a time watches the control vanish for no visible reason. Entering the mode dims the rest and puts the arrows in the slot the depth value occupies, so row heights do not change.

**The form is the dive detail you can type into** (designed after M1d, for the same reason the rest of §0.6 was designed after M1b: the screen was built to §2.2's structure and then styled by default, and it ended up speaking a different visual language from the rest of the app for identical content — a depth typed in Archivo and read back in Plex Mono). The form borrows the detail screen's grammar rather than inventing one:

- **A field is a row, not a box.** Label at the leading edge in Archivo 15 muted — the detail screen's own row label — and the value trailing. Separated by a hairline on each row's **top** edge, the same rule dive rows follow. Five bordered boxes down the core strip was the heaviest chrome in the app, drawn in advance for every field whether or not it was being used.
- **Figures in mono, names in sans.** A depth, duration, pressure or temperature is a data figure and takes Plex Mono 15 with tabular figures (§0.2); a site, centre or buddy is a name and stays Archivo. The unit follows the figure as a muted suffix, exactly as `12.2 m` reads on the detail — **and stays there while the field is empty**, so the row still says what belongs in it. It used to be the empty field's placeholder until 2026-11-14, when a phone showed what that costs: a right-aligned empty input draws its caret exactly where a right-aligned placeholder ends, so focusing an empty `min` row put the caret through the `n`.
- **Focus is what draws the affordance — a card** (redrawn 2026-11-14 to the owner's design canvas). The focused field becomes a `surface` card, radius 12, standing 8 in from each screen edge, and the hairline on its top edge goes, because a filled card with a rule along its top reads as a seam. Nothing else is drawn, so the box still appears where it is wanted instead of five times over. **The card's geometry is worn at rest too** — a negative margin out to its edge and a padding straight back to `CONTENT_INSET` — so focusing changes the fill and nothing else: the label and value do not move by a point under the finger that just touched them, and the row does not grow (the canvas draws 52; the row keeps 48 rather than shift everything below it on every tap). A picker that is open and the position row while it is locating are that field's focus and wear the same card. It replaced a full-bleed `surface` band, which read as a highlighted stripe across the screen rather than as the field you are in.
- **The whole row is the field** (2026-11-14). A press anywhere on the card that no control inside it takes — the label above all — focuses the input. The label was plain text, so the leading third of every row was dead to a tap, wider in Czech.
- **The return key walks the form** (2026-11-14). A single-line text field's key reads *next* and lands in the next text field on screen, in the order the form already declares (`CORE_STRIP_FIELDS`, then `FORM_GROUPS` in `FORM_GROUP_IDS` order) and over pickers, chip rows and collapsed groups; past the last it puts the keyboard away. It used to close the keyboard on every row. Notes keeps its return key for new lines. The numeric fields walk too: on iOS 26 a decimal pad whose field asks for *next* grows a floating **Next** key (seen on the simulator — the pad had no key at all before, which is what M3m's lifted save button was answering).
- **The focused field stays above the keyboard** (2026-11-14). With the keyboard up the save button rides on the keys and leaves about half the screen; the form scrolls so the focused field *and its suggestion list* fit in that half — lifted just far enough, or, when the list is taller than the half, with the field at the top and the list running down from it. The canvas's own words: suggestions "must fit above it, never scroll under". Found when the fourth suggestion for *K* sat behind the save button.
- **A field being edited has no clear control** (issue #29, owner's call, 2026-11-15, undoing 2026-11-14's ring). The canvas draws one inside the focused field, and here it pushed the right-aligned value 60 pt left on every focus and back on every blur, so *Next* moved two rows at once. The form's values are short, and holding backspace empties them. **A password is left to each platform's own secure field**, and Ponor adds no rule to it: iOS's empties on the first backspace after the diver comes back to it — they cannot see what a press would take off — and takes a character per press while they type; Android's takes a character per press. A carried row keeps its own ring — throwing away what carry-over filled in is a different act — and so do the optional pickers and the position row, which have no keyboard to backspace with. The caret and selection are ink rather than the platform's blue, which was the only hue anywhere in the app's chrome.
- **A group header is a cluster label** — Plex Mono 10.5, uppercase, +0.14 em, muted. *Conditions* and *Gas & cylinders* name the same groups on both screens and used to carry two different treatments.
- **A field error is text, not a field.** Muted, trailing, under the row it belongs to. Shipped once as a white rounded box the same height as an input, which read as a second empty field rather than a message.
- **A row whose value is *taken* rather than typed shows a call to action, not a placeholder.** `DateTimeField` reads *"Not set"* because a diver knows what a date is and how to give one. Nothing about an empty coordinate row says that a tap asks the device where you are, so it reads **"Use my location"** until it holds a pin (M2l); and an empty *Site* or *Centre* reads **"Choose a site"** / **"Choose a centre"**, the name of the page it opens (issue #19). The distinction is whether the empty state has to teach the gesture.
- **Buddy and guide suggest from history directly under the focused field — since issue #19 the only fields that do** (styled 2026-11-14, when the hold on it — "waits for M2, which reworks site search" — ran out and the owner's design canvas became the design). The list sits **under the card, on the screen's own ground**, not inside the fill. Each suggestion is a full-bleed row with a hairline on its top edge: the name in **ink**, Archivo Medium 16 — it was muted, on the grounds that an offer is not yet a value, and a column of muted names with nothing under them read as disabled text — with what the diver has done with it muted beneath (`4 dives · last 18 kvě 2026`), under a cluster label (`YOUR BUDDIES · 2`). The figures are `suggestionHistory`'s (domain/suggest.ts), counted the logbook's way — logged dives only, through `logbookStats` — and a name only planned dives carry shows no line rather than `0 dives`.
  **This bullet described the Site and Centre rows too until #19, and what it said about them moved or went.** A site's trailing deepest dive now trails a picker row, still unbanded (M1l: a deepest-of-a-set takes no band, where the canvas colours it). The create offer under the row is the picker's *Create* row. And the canvas's *near you* group — recorded here as "not built — nothing in v1 knows what is near" — is built, because the owner pulled *nearby* into v1 with the redesign (§9); its *log the position now* hatch is still not drawn, since a new place's position is asked for on that place's own page.
- **The Site and Centre rows are not fields; each opens a page** (issue #19). The row keeps the form's anatomy — label leading, the hairline on its top edge, the 48 floor — and **the whole row is the press**, label included, as on every other row. The value trails in a name's face (Archivo Medium 16), wrapping and never truncated; empty, it is the call to action above, in muted ink. Paired, a muted mono line under the name says where its catalogue row lives: the row's country as the **stored ISO code**, as the app's other site and centre rows show it, or **on this phone only** under a guest's row, which nothing pushes until they sign in. A signed-in diver's row still waiting to go up reads its country — a pending push is §7.5's indicator's business, not this row's — and a name with no id, or a row this device does not hold or that knows no country, has no line. **The ring stands at the trailing edge whenever the row holds a place, picked or carried** — a place that could only be replaced and never emptied would make a mistaken pick permanent — and beside a carried value it wears the return mark, as on any carried row. It empties name and id together and leaves `— cleared` only where the value was carried: emptying your own value is an edit, not a carry-over thrown away. One press opens one picker, for the two rows together.

**The site and centre picker is a page the form pushes** (2026-11-15, issue #19; its prototype is the Claude design "Ponor site picker", and §10 says where the build departs from it). `/pick/site` and `/pick/center` stand over the form, which stays mounted with everything typed in it; a pick comes back through `navigation/placePick.ts`. Top to bottom:

- **The way out and the title.** `‹ Cancel`, in the app's one treatment for leaving, changes nothing, and swipe-back does the same; then *Choose a site* / *Choose a centre* in the heading every screen title wears.
- **The *Selected* line, whenever the field holds something** — a small muted check, *Selected:* muted, the name in ink, Archivo 14, wrapping under itself rather than clipping. The owner's wording; *"on this dive"* was rejected as unclear.
- **The search, at the top** — a `surface` field 48 tall with a 12 radius, the magnifier leading and the form's clear ring trailing once it holds text, focused on open so the keyboard is up. **A deliberate difference from the Dives search**, which docks its field on the keyboard (above). It capitalises the first letter only and never autocorrects, because what it holds can become a new place's name — in a catalogue divers everywhere read, and most languages do not title-case a place (owner's call, §10).
- **A row of pills under it — *Near me* alone until the map arrives (#30).** A 36 pill with a hairline outline inside a 48 dp box, **inverted while on** — the chosen thing is the inverted thing — and reading *Locating…* while the device answers. A refusal is a muted sentence under the pills.
- **One list, its groups headed by cluster labels**: `DIVED HERE` (a centre's: `DIVED WITH`), `OTHER SITES`, `NEAR YOU · WITHIN 50 KM`, `NEAR SHARK POINT`, and `OR` over *No centre*. A group is counted while something is typed (`OTHER SITES · 3`) **except *Near you***, whose heading already states its bound. Each place is a 64 row with a hairline on its top edge: the name in ink, Archivo Medium 16, over a mono 11.5 muted line — `4 dives · last 18 May 2026` (a centre adds its sites), `community · HR`, `added by you`, `on this phone only · not shared yet`, `dived · 4 dives`. A row the query found by another name it goes by says so first — `also called Stone Gate · community · HR` (#31; `withAlsoCalled`, format/display.ts), as a row in the Sites directory does. Under *Near Shark Point* the line says where the place came from, as under *Other* — the heading names the site and the distance trails the row, so the line says neither again (owner's call).
- **What trails a row.** A dived site's deepest dive in Plex Mono, **in ink and never in a band colour** — an aggregate (M1l). A distance on either Near list, muted. **Nothing on a centre's *Dived with* row**: no figure is true of a centre. The canvas draws a muted `used` in that slot on its *Your centres* row; it is left out on purpose, because the *Dived with* heading already says it (owner's call, §10). **The place the field holds carries a check in place of its figure** wherever it appears; it is never added to a group it does not belong to, so a place created a moment ago — which has no dives — is shown with nothing typed by the *Selected* line alone.
- ***Create "vis"*** closes the list whenever something is typed: the typed text in quotes, a muted `+` trailing, and a meta line saying what comes next (`new site · details next`; for a guest, `kept on this phone until you sign in`). ***No centre*** — *you dived without one*, a muted `—` trailing — closes the centre picker's list while nothing is typed and *Near me* is off.
- **A sentence where rows would be**: `Nothing within 50 km.` (or `… matches "vis".`), said only once the catalogue has answered **and the server has answered about the fix** — until then *Looking for places within 50 km…*, since the community's rows may be on their way; out of signal the server answers at once. A read that failed says so instead and never also calls the catalogue empty. A page with no place to list — no dives yet, nothing typed, *Near me* off — opens on *Type a name to find a site or create one.* (the Search screen's empty-field prompt, here), above the centre picker's *Or · No centre*; a blank page under a search gave a new diver no way in.

**The create pages are the form's grammar on a page of their own** — *New dive site* / *New dive centre* (`/site/new`, `/center/new`), pushed from *Create*:

- `‹ Back` returns to the picker with the query intact. The title, and under it in muted mono *the name is the only thing required* — and, for a guest only, *Kept on this phone. It joins the community map when you sign in.* That line, the *Create* row's meta, the picker's provenance on a guest's own row (*on this phone only · not shared yet*), the dive form's caption under a paired one (*on this phone only*) and the site and centre directories' empty state (*It stays on this phone until you sign in.*) are the only places the app says anything to a guest about it — no banner, no prompt.
- **Name** is a form row, prefilled with what was typed, wearing the focused card, and capitalising its first letter only, as the search does. A centre's **Address** stands directly under it, as the canvas draws it (issue #31): one optional line, never autocorrected — a street the keyboard does not know is still the street.
- **Description** comes next on both pages (#31): a stacked multi-line row with no cap on its length, *optional* until something is written, captioned ***everyone can read it***. It is published with the place, and it is what a diver opens a place to learn, so it sits at the top rather than in a group.
- **Position** is a cluster label over a card — the *Show on map* card's look (`MapCard`, on the dive detail, site and centre pages), one style definition (`placeCard`) — whose glyph and *Use current position* ask the device on a press; under them, muted, what a press does, *Locating…*, or the coordinates once the page holds a position. *from this dive* trails the label while the position is the dive's own pin. **No accuracy figure** (§10).
- **Country** stands under the position card, read-only: the ISO code derived from the position, in mono, or a muted `—` while there is no position. A centre with an address and no position reads its address's country instead (§2.3's one exception) as `HR · from the address`, the source at the canvas's 0.6 opacity of the code's own ink (`newPlaceCountrySource`) and no colour of its own; it is asked as the diver leaves the *Address* box and shown only while the box still holds the address it answers. **It is also what is saved**: Save hands that answer, with the point it is for, to `addSite` / `addCenter`, which ask the geocoder themselves only when it is missing or about another point — once, rather than a second time on Save (up to four seconds more offline). An address's answer goes the same way with the address it is for, and a lookup still out for the address in the box is waited for rather than asked again.
- **The rest sit in collapsed groups** (#31), the dive form's group grammar (§2.2) — a cluster-label header and its chevron — under *Country*. A site has ***The site***: the dive's **Water body** and **Salinity** — in that order since #53, the salinity under the water body that sets it and not drawn at all for the ocean — moved in from the top and still the form's own `OptionChips`, and **Entry**, a set since the owner's call of 2026-11-17 (`TokenChips`, captioned *any that apply* — tiles with their drawings since #6, as the form's single entry is, and *other* a word), each of the three with *from this dive* trailing while it holds the dive's value — on *Entry* in the caption's place, while the set is still the dive's one way in; then *Site type* (a set, captioned *any that apply*), *Suitable for*, *Site depth*, a figure in mono with the diver's unit trailing — **the unit, not the canvas's *optional***, by the 2026-11-14 rule above that a unit stays on an empty row and never shares it with a placeholder — and *Altitude* straight after it, the same (a height above sea level, below it allowed, saved to the whole metre; **pressing *Use current position* offers the fix's altitude and never writes it** (the owner's call, 2026-11-17, §10): on iOS, when the receiver vouches for it (a vertical accuracy above 0 and at most 50 m), a quiet outlined pill stands right-aligned under the row — *Use GPS altitude · 12 m* / *Použít výšku z GPS · 12 m*, the figure whole in the diver's unit with its unit, in *+ Add a name*'s pill (`outlinePill`), a button labelled by its words — and a tap fills the row with that figure and the pill goes. Ignored, nothing is saved from it; a value the diver typed stays until the pill is tapped; the pill is hidden while the row already holds that value (what Save would write is the same). Each later press replaces it with its own fix's altitude, and a fix with none takes it away; a refused press leaves it. The press opens *The site* when it brings an offer, so the pill is never out of sight. No pill on Android, for a fix with no altitude, or for a pin handed over from the dive); and *Wreck name* and *Year sunk* only while *Wreck* is one of the types. And ***Other names***: one row to type in, and under it a ***+ Add a name*** pill that adds another — the picker's *Near me* pill in its 48 box, one definition (`outlinePill`), as the canvas draws it. A centre has ***Contact*** — *Website*, moved in from the top with its URL keyboard, *Phone* on the phone keypad, *Email*, *Opening hours* — and ***Services*** — *Services* and *Training agencies*, both sets. **A centre's *Phone*, *Email* and *Address* opt out of AutoFill** in so many words — `textContentType` `none`, `autoComplete` `off` and, on Android, `importantForAutofill` `no` — because they are the shop's and published to every diver, and a field that merely carries no hint still lets Android's Autofill offer the diver's own by its input type. It is an explicit opt-out and no more: whatever the platform or the keyboard still suggests on its own, the app can neither see nor stop.
- **A closed group says what it holds**, in muted mono between its title and its chevron — `water, entry, type, depth` (the first rows, in their order; 28 characters fit, so not every row), `names it also goes by`, `website, phone, email, hours`, `services, agencies` — and drops the line once open, where the rows say it themselves. **A group opens when it holds a value** (§2.2: *no data, closed; data carried, open*), decided as the page opens, which on a page that starts empty means *The site* when the dive handed it water or an entry. Unlike the dive form's, the groups are not remembered: the page is opened per place, and nothing about one place says how the next is filled. A closed group still saves what it holds.
- **A set is a row of chips that each toggle** (`TokenChips`): `OptionChips`' look and styles, but a press adds or removes one member, and a screen reader hears each chip as a checkbox rather than a button. A token this build does not offer is kept, never dropped by a press beside it (§10's store-and-flag).
- **Save site** / **Save centre** is the inverted primary button in the form's footer, lifted with the keyboard and dimmed while there is no name. §2.3's matches stand above it in the form's suggestion-row treatment, outside the scroll so a diver who pressed Save sees them, and the button then reads *Save as a new site anyway*. A failed write is a muted sentence in the same place. **What Save writes is what is on screen** (#31): a blank row as `null`, a site depth typed in the diver's units as metres, an altitude as whole metres, a year as the digits it was typed in, the wreck's name and year only while *Wreck* is a type, and the other names clean (`cleanAltNames`, domain/search.ts: trimmed, no blank, no repeat, none that folds to the name itself) — which is also what the duplicate check asks about.
- **Each match is the sentence over a muted line** (owner's call, PR #35) — *Did you mean “Shark Point”?*, *“Kotelna” is already a site.*, or since #31 *“Kamena vrata” is another name for Stone Gate.*, as `twinRelation` relates the two (§2.3), and *Did you mean “Stone Gate” (also called Kamena vrata)?* for a guess the server made through another name — and the line is its country, then how far it is from the page's position — `HR · 120 m away` — with the distance left out when either place has no position, and the line when neither half is known. **A tap opens that site's own page** (`/site/[id]`, carrying the form's token as `pick`), so a diver can check it really is the same place; that page then ends in **Use this site**, the inverted primary button in the form's footer, which picks it through the same hand-off as a list pick and closes it, the create page and the picker. `‹ Back` there returns to the create page exactly as it was left — name, chips, position and the question still standing. The page carries the button only when opened from a match whose request is still waiting. **No chevron**, though the row navigates: the rule below spends the chevron on in-place disclosure and never on navigation, and #5 decides how a pressable row shows itself (§10).

**A site's page and a centre's page say what the place is before what the diver did there** (issue #31, in the order picked on the canvas). Top to bottom: the name and the diver's own summary line; **the description**, a paragraph in full ink, Archivo 16 on 23 (`catalogueDescription` — a new value), its newlines the author's and nothing cutting it short; *Show on map*; the place's facts; and the diver's own dives there. Divers' reviews (#43) will stand between the map card and the facts — the order was settled with them in it — and nothing is drawn for them until they exist. Every fact is shown only when it is filled (§1: a sparse row is normal), and a cluster with nothing to show is not drawn.

- **A site's facts are one cluster, *The site*, with a note under it** (the owner's pick C, 2026-11-17; tiles since #6). It opens with **tiles**, the dive detail's look (§0.6's table): each site type, the water body and every way in, each its drawing over its word and its field's name (*Wreck · Site type*, *Ocean · Water body*, *Boat · Entry*). Then the rows: *Suitable for*, *Site depth*, *Altitude*, *Wreck* (`Teti · sunk 1930`, the year never grouped as a figure would be), *Salinity*, *Other names*, *Country*. **The note names only the rows the site itself sets** — *Salinity, Water body and Entry fill in when you pick this site for a dive, over anything already there.*, two joined by *and*, or *Entry fills…* for one; Czech's *se vyplní* is one form for all — decided by the prefill's own reading (`siteDefaultValue`, §2.1), so the page and the prefill cannot disagree: *Salinity* and *Water body* each when it is set, *Entry* only when the site offers exactly one way in, counted in words as its tiles are (`wordsOf`, #69: a blank or a number beside *Shore* is no second way in). Nothing to name, no note. It names each by the label it is drawn with, in muted mono at 11.5 (`siteFactsNote` — the canvas's), and says what the pick overrides — *anything already there*, since #53 a value the diver typed included, on a new dive and on a stored one moved to the site — naming the pick as the moment because opening a dive already logged there never refills it (the owner's wording, 2026-11-24; until then it promised only *over anything carried from your last dive*, the one tier the site then outranked). A salinity that follows the water body the site sets is the water body's doing, not the site's: the note does not name it, and the form captions it *from the water body* where it lands. A site depth stays in ink, as the page always drew it and the canvas draws it.
- **A centre's facts are *Contact*** — *Address*, *Phone* (in mono: a figure), *Email*, *Opening hours*, *Website*, *Country* — **and *Services*** — *Services* and *Agencies*. The address is a row there and not part of the header line the canvas's screen 12 draws (the owner's call), and *Country* moved into *Contact* as the canvas has it, so the old *Centre* cluster went.
- **A set reads as a line of text, not chips** — `Nitrox · Rental` (`formatPlaceTokens`), and a site's ways in on its *Entry* row, `Shore · Boat` — and a token a newer client wrote reads as a word, never as its translation key. **A member that is not a word is skipped**: a number, a `null` or a blank string, which another client's row may hold since the JSON decoder vouches for the list and not what is in it. A word with spaces around it is that word (` boat` is *Boat*, with its drawing), and a set with no word left draws no row. One guard does this for every stored list a diver reads: a place's sets, the site's tiles, the prefill's ways in, a place's other names, and a dive's equipment on its page and in the CSV export (`wordsOf` in `domain/words.ts`, #69). So none of them can draw a trailing separator, an empty row or a tile with no word, and the page and the prefill count a site's ways in alike.
- **The address, the number and the email are each a fact that is also a link**, as the website row already was: the address opens the device's maps searching it (`mapsSearchUrl`), the number calls, the email writes. **No mark says so** — how a pressable row shows itself is #5's, for every such row at once — and each announces what a press will do (*Call …*, *Write to …*). A phone or an email that is not a number or one plain email address (`ask at the bar`, `dive@`) stays a fact with nothing to press (`platform/openContact.ts`, §4.1).

**The empty logbook is the one screen that can teach §0.1** (M1h, the owner's design). It said "Your logbook is empty." over a button — the only thing it had to say, and nothing a diver did not already know. Meanwhile the rule the whole palette rests on was explained **nowhere**: a diver met the depth colours one number at a time at the right-hand end of a dive row, where a colour that happens to vary looks like decoration. This is the only screen with **no dive to attach a colour to**, so it is the only place the scale can appear *as a scale*, and it is the last time it is seen out of context. Top to bottom, left-aligned in the app's one content column (§0.6's `CONTENT_INSET`): the large title with **"0 dives"** beneath it in muted mono — which is also what tells this branch apart from the one that has not read the logbook yet — then the mark at 120, `NOTHING LOGGED YET` in the cluster label, §1's *works at sea* said to a diver rather than to a planner, **six bars in the six band colours with their ranges beneath**, and two mono lines giving the rule its reason: colour is depth, and water strips warm light first.

  **The prose takes a measure, and the mark takes its air below rather than around** (M1l, owner's call). Both prose blocks are capped at a reading width instead of running the full screen; a long line of 11 pt mono edge to edge reads as a paragraph of terms and conditions, which is the opposite of what a first-run screen is for. The measure is a width, never a hand-placed line break — Czech runs 20–30 % longer (§0.5) and would break anywhere a break was put. The extra clearance under the mark is set **on the mark**, not by widening the block's rhythm: the block is centred, so a bigger shared gap moves everything and closes nothing in particular.

Three things about it are load-bearing rather than stylistic. **The mark is monochrome here, and that is §0.1 enforcing itself** — §0.3 strokes it in the depth gradient on the *icon*, but on screen a gradient mark would be colour spent on brand, so the only hue anywhere on this screen belongs to the legend, and the legend is depth. **Every number comes from `theme/depth.ts`**, the bars' ranges and the two figures in the reason line alike; a legend that retypes a boundary is the drift §4.1 exists to stop, and it was already visible before it was fixed — bars reading `0–20 … 131+ ft` under a caption saying "6 m". And **the labels follow the diver's units**, ragged feet included, because a first-run screen that quietly switched unit systems would be a strange thing to teach on.

**The empty logbook also offers an account, under the first dive and never in front of it** (issue #24, the owner's design on the canvas's screen 02). For a guest — a build with a backend, once the session read has answered, nobody signed in (`offersAccount`, the one owner of that rule, asked of each screen's own session read) — the screen reads top to bottom: the teaching block, now **top-aligned** so the air falls under the scale; *Log your first dive*, still the one inverted control; an **`or` rule** (two hairlines, a muted mono word); and the **offer card** (`AccountOfferCard`): *Better with an account*, *Totally optional — Ponor works fully without one.*, three glyph words — *Backups · Every device · Community* — and an outlined *Sign in or create an account*. Signed in, before the session read, or on a build with no backend, there is no rule and no card and the button is back at the bottom. **Its title promises no backup**, because on this screen there is nothing to back up yet (the owner turned down *Back up with an account* for saying so). **Nothing to dismiss**: §1 makes the account optional, so the card stands while the diver is a guest and leaves with the first dive. It is also the way back for a diver who already has an account — on a new phone, or after signing out, this is the screen they see. The promise line no longer says *No account, no upload*, which the card would contradict; it reads *Ponor keeps every dive on this phone and works with the boat out of signal* for everyone. **§0.5 is bent here, knowingly**: with the card under it the primary sits mid-screen rather than in the bottom third — accepted for this one screen, which a diver sees once and mostly on land. The three words wrap rather than clip, for Czech. **With the offer, the button, the rule and the card scroll with the teaching block**, pinned to the bottom of the content (review of PR #52): pinned outside the scroll they are ~260 pt in Czech and grow with the diver's text size, and at a large size on a 4.7" phone they outgrew the screen and put the card's button under the tab bar. Where everything fits, the screen is the same; without the offer the button stays pinned outside the scroll, as M1h set. **And when the button would start below the fold, the screen teaches less** (review round 2 of PR #52, the owner's call) — a deliberate exception to M1h's teaching block, for a guest only: the two reason lines go first, then the mark, one step per measurement of the screen it actually drew, never back up from a trimmed layout (which would flicker), and in full again only when the viewport or the text size changes. The label, the promise and the legend's six bars stay at every step: the bars are what still say colour is depth. The first frame is drawn in full before any trimming, and that one-frame jump is accepted rather than an invisible measuring pass; if the button is still below the fold with both gone, the screen stops there and scrolls. **A new text size remounts the teaching scroll**: re-rendering it in place while iOS applied a new Dynamic Type size left the texts that stayed with their old heights (found on the simulator), and a fresh mount measures every text at the new size.

**The same card heads Settings for a guest** (screen 21), above *Units* — where a phone puts its own account — and while it shows, Settings' *Account* row is not drawn: one way in at a time (the owner's call on PR #52, over keeping both or putting the card in the Account section). Signed in, there is no card and the row is as it was. **Both screens move once when the session read answers, and that is accepted** (review of PR #52): before it, Settings draws the row and no card and the first-run screen draws no offer, and for a guest the card arrives with the answer — a keychain read, normally within the first frames. Holding the card's slot until then would make a signed-in diver's screen jump instead, when the empty slot collapsed; and drawing the card before the answer is the offer to a signed-in diver that M1f's rule forbids.

**The account screen wears the canvas's grouped layout** (screens 19–20, the owner's pick of two drawn options, 2026-11-16: the screen was "boring", and its inputs were "hard to distinguish" from its buttons). Every state opens with one head — the mark at half strength (the first-run mark, 44 wide), a **28 pt bold title**, and one line: *Dives already on this phone come with you.* on sign in and new account (that the account is optional was said by the card that led here), what the account does once signed in, the instruction on *Check your email*. Sign in and new account add the card's three as sentences beside their glyphs (`AccountPerks`, one list for both places). **Both rows sit in one `surface` card**, the form's row grammar inside it — label leading, value trailing, a hairline between rows rather than over them, no focus card inside a card (`FormField`'s `grouped`); a refusal still stands under the row it is about, inside the card. **Every second control is a button shape**: *Forgot password?* is §0.6's quiet pill under the card, the other mode is the outlined button under the primary, *Back to sign in* is the outlined button alone in the footer of *Check your email*, and *Sign out* is the outlined button at the end of the content. The way out reads **‹ Back**: the first-run card is a second way in, and M3k's rule lets a way out name a destination only when there is exactly one. Departures from the canvas, each kept by the owner on PR #52: the head and body sit on the app's 16 column (the canvas draws 20), the card's button is 48 tall (drawn 44 — §0.5's floor is a box here, never `hitSlop`), the footer keeps its hairline, and a refusal stands inside the card under its row (drawn under the card).

**The dive detail is the canvas's screens 03 and 14** (2026-11-14, issue #3; §10 has the departures). Top to bottom:

- **The top bar** is the way out at the leading edge and *Edit* and ⋯ as two 48 boxes at the trailing one, the group pulled 8 outboard of the column, as the canvas draws it: ⋯'s dots are centred in their box, so a box on the column would leave their ink 16 inboard of the edge every value shares, and the 8 halves that. With the device's safe area above it, this is the canvas's 96 (48 status pad + 48 target).
- **The hero is a dive row at detail scale**, literally: the dive number on a line of its own (`diveNumber`'s style, as in the row), the site at 20 on 22, then `date · time in · centre` in mono, and the 34 pt depth hung from the top of the block at the trailing edge. No rule beneath it; it closes on 18 of air. The time is the diver's own `time_in`, never a computed figure, because this line carries no `=`.
- **Cards are the things read as a whole**: a `surface` fill, radius 12, on the column with 14 beneath. *Show on map* holds the position — the map glyph, the words, the coordinates muted under them, 56 tall — in place of the GPS row, and opens Ponor's own map pointed at the dive (§3, issue #21) — pointed at a planned dive's site instead (the map does not draw a plan), and the device's maps only for a plan with no site. A site's and a centre's page carry the same card (`components/MapCard.tsx`) under their own lines, for a row with a position. §0.4's profile is the second card, padded 16 / 16 / 12. A pair of coordinates no map can draw (finite but off the globe) gets no card and stays the GPS row it was.
- **Rows are the form's rows**: 48, padding 12 / 16, the hairline on each row's **top** edge, running to the screen's real edges with the column held by the row's own padding — so the first row of a cluster draws the rule under its label and the last row has nothing beneath it. A cluster label sits 22 below the rows above it and 6 above its own first row; the first cluster sits flush under the hero or a card, which close on their own air, but not under the `Imported from` row, which does not. The computed `=` stands in a 14 pt slot of its own, flush left, so the air is on the value's side.
- **A row that navigates is the same row**: a paired *Site* or *Centre* is 48 like every other, which is §0.5's floor, so it needs nothing added. Nothing on the screen yet says which rows open a page — that is #5's, and §10 says what the uniform height cost.
- **The record's own actions are behind ⋯**, in a sheet: 8 in from three edges, radius 22, on `sheet` over a `scrim`; a header naming the dive (`DIVE #N` in the cluster label's voice, site and date muted beside it — a plan says *Planned dive*); each action a 64 card on `sheet-item` with what it costs under its name; *Cancel* at 52 in the app's one treatment for leaving. The destructive act is last and never coloured; a hairline separates it from the actions above it once there are any. Today Delete stands alone. The platform's confirmation rises over the sheet, and the sheet closes only on a yes.

**Option chips and the group header** (owner's calls, after seeing the form built):

- **A chip is filled.** `surface` behind an unselected chip, `action` ink behind the selected one — the same invert the save control uses, so "the chosen thing is the inverted thing" is one rule across the app. This does put a `surface` fill on two different things (a chip, and the focused row); they are told apart by shape and scale rather than by colour, a small pill inside a row against a card around the whole row. Recorded as a known trade-off, not an oversight.
- **An icon appears only where the value has one, and the test is whether the mark carries the meaning or merely labels it.** *Shore* and *boat* pass trivially. The first version of this paragraph ruled out the scales — salinity, suits, cylinder materials — on the grounds that drawn as icons they collapse into near-identical droplets and suits separated by tally marks, which is a legend, and §10's computed-value square is the precedent: a symbol that needs a legend has already failed. The icon **supplements the label rather than replacing it** — never an icon alone. **Only *Entry* carries marks today**; every other chip row is bare.

  **M1h gave the scales marks that passed this test on paper, and M1i took them out after seeing them.** They were not arbitrary glyphs: current arrows accumulated one way, surge two ways, visibility bars counted up, weather took SF Symbols' own skies — each encoding its scale in itself, which is exactly what the paragraph above asks for. **They came out on measurement, not on principle** (owner's call): each arrow cost ≈29 px, which pushed *Current* and *Surge* onto a second line that they clear comfortably without one, and *Visibility low* — a single bar at that size — read as **punctuation**, a full stop sitting in front of the word. A mark that passes the meaning test and still costs a row its line has failed a test the paragraph did not name. **The intent survives on §9's shelf as a drawn set of our own**, which is the owner's actual objection: SF Symbols is not this app's hand. Do not restore these from the shelf one row at a time — a set is the point.

  One argument from that round is worth keeping, because it is the reasoning any replacement has to survive: **Waves shipped bare even when the others had marks.** Repetition encodes *frequency*, not amplitude, so a counted Waves would have made Waves, Current and Surge three rows of "three of something"; and the only working amplitude encoding was Visibility's bars, which reused would have made **both** rows a legend. There was no mark that carried the meaning, so there was none — the rule applied against its own convenience, which is the hard direction.

  **A mark takes the ink of the label beside it, handed out by the chip.** `OptionChips` knows whether it is selected and therefore what ink its label is about to wear; the mark is given that value rather than choosing one, which is what makes a mark invert with its chip instead of vanishing on it. The rule's second half — that a **drawn** mark resolves that ink back to a sheet style rather than painting it directly, since §4.1 gives `theme/styles.ts` every token-to-property binding — lost its call site when the scale marks went and **holds again since issue #6**: `OptionChips` and `TokenChips` read the ink and the ground off their own chip or tile style and hand both to the drawn icon (`drawnIcon`), and `EntryIcon`'s SF Symbols retired with it. The fields whose values have drawings are **tile rows** (`layout="tiles"`): the icon over the word, three to a row (waves four), the same control and the same invert as a chip, drawn as a tile.

  **§9's set has arrived, drawn for this app** (issue #6, the owner's picks on the design canvas, category by category, 2026-11-16/17). **The hand:** line drawings on a 40 × 40 grid, one ink, no fills; a main stroke of 2.0 and a detail stroke of 1.35, round caps and joins, the Dives tab mark's hand. The water scenes share one grid so a row of them reads as a set: a 3 pt margin with nothing running off the tile, the water surface at y 22, the bed near y 35, and the same surface wave and sub-ripple wherever there is water. A shape may be **knocked out**, filled with the ground the icon sits on so the lines behind it disappear; a knock is the tile's own colour, never a second ink, which is why whoever draws an icon hands it the ground as well as the ink. **One owner:** the drawings are data (`components/icons/conditionIconData.ts`), `ConditionIcon` is the only thing that paints them, and `conditionIconFor` is the only thing that decides which value has one.

  | Field | A drawing | Words only |
  |---|---|---|
  | Entry | shore, boat | other |
  | Water body | ocean, lake, river, quarry, cave, pool | |
  | Weather | sunny, cloudy, overcast, rainy, windy, foggy | |
  | Waves | flat, small, medium, large: one per level, height is the scale | |
  | Visibility | one for the field, the diving mask | the level |
  | Current | one for the field (streamlines); *None* is the same lines dashed, without heads | the level |
  | Surge | one for the field (two swing arcs); *None* dashed, without heads | the level |
  | Cylinder configuration | single, twinset, sidemount (the harness) | |
  | Suit | shorty, wet, semidry, dry (a hood on semidry and dry) | none |
  | Site type | wreck, reef, wall, pinnacle, canyon, swim-through, muck (the seahorse), drift, blue water | |
  | Salinity, material, weighting, suitable for | | all |

  A token this build does not know (§10's store-and-flag) and a scale level out of range have no drawing and keep their word. Two **figure glyphs** in the same hand say which depth a number is: *max depth* (an arrow from the surface to the floor) and *average depth* (the arrow stops at a level halfway, the floor below only dashed). They are never curves, which §0.4 keeps for recorded profiles. They are drawn at 24 in the rows' label ink (`figureGlyph`, one owner: `DepthGlyph`). Times and temperatures get none, since a clock or a thermometer would only repeat the label.

  **The drawn rating now has two homes and stays one component.** §0.6 has specified drawn circles since M1c and the dive row has drawn them since; the form now uses the same one rather than a second — §4.1's whole point. In the form each dot is additionally its own control and carries its own 48 dp target (§0.5), because there a dot is something you press. SF Symbols through `expo-symbols` with a Material Symbol on Android, exactly as `SearchCapsule`'s magnifier already works.
- **The chevron marks in-place disclosure — on a group header and on a field row alike — and nothing else.** A collapsible group is marked by a chevron, not by the words "Show"/"Hide". **Drawn, not typed** — the same reason §0.6 already gives for rating marks: a glyph's size varies by typeface, so a typed chevron looks broken somewhere. It rotates to show state, needs no translation, and drops a word from a row that is otherwise pure structure.

  **M1h extended the mark to a field row** — the cylinder summary hides its spec fields exactly as a group header hides its rows — and the axis that makes that coherent is worth stating, because the obvious wording is wrong.
