It is *not* "a field whose value is read rather than typed": that describes `DateTimeField`, which deliberately carries **no** chevron and marks its open state with the `surface` fill instead. The real distinction is **what the gesture does to the page**. A control that **discloses further rows in place** carries the chevron; one that **opens a picker over the row** does not. Two riders follow: the mark is never spent on navigation — a row that takes you somewhere else is not disclosing anything — and the style is named for the rule rather than for its first caller (`disclosureChevron`, not `formGroupChevron`).

Unchanged, and deliberately: the save control stays filled inverted ink (§10), and the Logged/Planned chip and `‹ Cancel` keep the treatments agreed during M1d. This sentence used to end by preserving the `carried ×` chip, **which contradicted the carry-over paragraph three above it** — M1h replaced that chip, that paragraph says so, and this one went on protecting it for two milestones. Removed rather than reworded: a spec that argues with itself is worse than one that is merely out of date, and this is the second time §0.6 has been caught asserting something no screen draws.

---

## 1. Product & principles

Ponor is a hobby project, published free, for divers who find existing log apps fiddly. It replaces the paper logbook for manual logging: dive count, sites, conditions, gas, gear, and notes. It is not (yet) a dive-computer companion — the schema leaves room for that later. There is deliberately **no trip entity** to create or manage: carry-over prefill covers a trip's shared details, and the dive list groups itself into trips by date and place.

- **Log a dive in under a minute.** Every new dive is prefilled from the previous one. Logging dive #2 and #3 of a trip means changing the depth, the time, and the pressures — nothing else.
- **Only the fields you use.** Everything is optional except the date. Field groups collapse, and any group can be hidden permanently in settings.
- **The map grows itself.** Dive sites and centers are a shared, community-built database. Autocomplete keeps names consistent; fuzzy matching stops duplicates; optional GPS pins put dives on a map.
- **Works at sea.** The whole app runs offline from on-device SQLite. An account is only needed to back up, sync a second device, and contribute named sites — and syncing happens by itself.

## 2. The fast-logging system

The core of the product. Four mechanisms work together.

### 2.1 Carry-over prefill

**Carried over from the last dive:** dive centre and site · entry (shore/boat) · salinity and water body · cylinders (material, configuration, size, working pressure) · gas mixture per cylinder (O₂/He %) · weights · suit and its thickness · equipment (hood, gloves, boots, torch, camera) · buddy and guide.

**Fresh every dive:** max and average depth · duration · time in · starting **and** ending pressure · visibility, both the scale and the distance · temperatures · weather · waves/current/surge · how the weighting felt · rating · title · notes · the exact GPS point (`latitude`/`longitude`).

The three fields M1h added to this half — weather, the visibility scale and the weighting judgement — are fresh for the same reason the pressures are, stated below: they describe the conditions of *one* dive, and a carried-over value that looks like data and is not is the failure this split exists to prevent.

The GPS point is fresh for the same reason the pressures are: the *site* carries, which is the right granularity, but the exact entry position is a claim of precision that a stale value would make falsely. A carried-over pressure or pin looks like data and is not. **The rule was unexercised for one milestone and is not any more** (M2l). It was written expecting §2.3's *map* to end that, and *use my location* did — worth noting because a plan that names which half of a sentence will land first is usually guessing.

- **Derived automatically:** the dive number (§2.5); the date stays on the previous dive's date when that dive was **today or yesterday** — never when it is dated ahead of today, because a dive that has not happened yet is not recent (§10); used pressure = start − end; surface interval from the previous dive; RMV in l/min when average depth, time, and cylinder size are present.
- **Site defaults:** picking a site prefills entry, salinity, and water body from the site's own defaults — and those win over carry-over when you switch sites. **A site's entry is a set** (#31) — every way in it offers, where a dive's is the one way it used — so it prefills the dive's entry **only when it offers exactly one**; with several, or none, it says nothing, as a `null` column does, and the entry is left to the diver and to carry-over. Choosing the dive's entry from a site's several is the dive form's, #63. **Site, water body and salinity are a hierarchy, and setting a parent sets its children** (#53, the owner's call of 2026-11-19, widened to the site on 2026-11-23). **A site sets what it knows** — its water body, its one way in, its salinity — over a value the diver set by hand; what it doesn't know keeps the diver's value; **carry-over** fills the rest. **Whatever sets the water body sets the salinity with it** — the diver tapping one, a site, or carry-over: the **ocean** is salt and its Salinity row is not drawn; any other water body takes the site's or the inherited salinity when it was recorded for that same water body (a site that says *Lake · Salt* fills in *Lake · Salt*, and a salt lake carries over as one), and otherwise **fresh**, captioned *from the water body* at the row's end. A salinity set by hand lasts until the water body changes. With no water body the row works as before. `salinityFor` and `siteDefaultFills` (domain/siteDefaults.ts) are the rule. This replaced M2s's three tiers, whose first — what the **diver typed** beats everything, *"overwriting a hand-entered value because someone then picked a site is the worst outcome available here"* — still holds for every row a site says nothing about; §10 records why it gave way for the rest. The latest gesture still wins: a row the diver touches while the site's row is being read is theirs when the answer lands. And **only a change of site counts**: re-picking the site the dive is already at — a stored dive's own, or the one carry-over brought — writes nothing (PR #80's review).
  **A `null` column means the catalogue does not know, not that the answer is empty**, so it supplies nothing. And **switching sites falls back to carry-over, never to the site you just left**: the fields are a function of the three inputs the dive has now, not an accumulation of every site visited during the edit — a path-dependent version would seed the *next* site created with a neighbouring site's defaults, published under the diver's name.
  **On a stored dive, only on the diver's own gesture** (#53). Changing a stored dive's site, or its water body, sets what the new parent knows, as on a new dive; opening one never refills it, and a correction to a site in the catalogue never rewrites the dives logged there — a stored dive's three columns are still that diver's record of the water they were in. (It was create mode only until #53; the reason it gave, the catalogue's correction overwriting the diver's record, is the part that still holds.)
- **Cylinder presets:** named cylinder sets ("twin 12 steel", "alu 80 nitrox") apply the whole cylinders-and-gas block in one tap. Scoped to the cylinders alone (owner's call, M1e — §10): a suit, weights and hood already carry from the previous dive without being named, and a preset that also carried them would be a second, staler way to fill the same fields.
- **The app learns:** pickers order options by your usage frequency, so your usual cylinder, weights, and suit sit on top without any setup.

### 2.2 Progressive form

One scrollable form. A small core strip is always visible: **date, site and centre** — what identifies the dive rather than what measures it. Everything else lives in seven groups, in this order:

| Group | Holds | |
|---|---|---|
| *Times & depth* | max depth · average depth · duration · time in | **opens by default** |
| *Gas & cylinders* | the cylinder spec · O₂ and He · **start and end pressure** | **opens by default** |
| *Conditions* | weather · water and air temperature · visibility and its distance · waves · current · surge | |
| *Water & entry* | entry · water body · salinity · GPS pin | |
| *Equipment* | suit and thickness · accessories · weights and how they felt | |
| *People* | buddy · guide | |
| *Notes & rating* | title · notes · rating | |

Only the date is required. **There is no "hide fields" setting** — the rule below makes one unnecessary (owner's call, M1i; §10).

**The pressures belong to the cylinder, and the order is what makes that bearable.** They were in the strip, and the owner's reason for wanting them back there is sound — he fills both on every dive. The reason they cannot go is multi-cylinder: a *Start pressure* row in the strip describes `tanks.0` and silently says nothing about the second cylinder, which is a strip lying about what it shows. **So the group moved instead of the fields** — *Gas & cylinders* sits second, above *Conditions*, opens by default, and the pressures are two rows further down the screen than they were, rather than behind a tap (owner's call, M1i).

**Conditions is what the day was like; where you are is a different question.** *Entry*, *salinity* and *water body* sat in *Conditions* and are not conditions — §2.1 gives all three away by prefilling them **from the site's own defaults**. They are properties of the place, they carry over, and they are touched about once a trip, so they get their own group below the one a diver actually fills. Weather leads *Conditions* because it is the first thing anyone notices about a dive day (owner's call, M1i).

**The form takes the pin rather than asking anyone to type it** (M1i removed the keypads, M2l gave the pair a row again). Two decimal keypads were an input nobody would ever use on a boat, and §2.3 has always said where a pin really comes from: the map, or *use my location*. So *Water & entry* carries **one GPS row** whose value is **taken from the device on a press** — the second of §2.3's two answers, and the one that arrived first. The columns never moved (§6) and the dive detail has been able to read a point back since M1a, against nothing that could write one.

**The core strip guessed twice.** It was fixed at date · site · centre · max depth · duration before anyone had logged a dive with it, which left *time in* and the two pressures behind a collapsed group — so surface interval, which the app computes and shows, had its only input hidden, and the diver who fills start/end on every single dive had to open a group on every single dive. Found by the owner using it. The second answer was an eight-field strip holding all of them — which fixed the hiding and flattened structure that was doing work. The third and current answer keeps the strip for identity and lets the groups that earn it start open (§10), so nothing is hidden and a diver who never records an average depth can collapse that group once and keep it collapsed.

**Groups remember themselves, and the memory holds three states, not two.** A group opens when the diver opened it last time **or** when this dive already has a value in it — the second half matters because carry-over fills groups nobody touched. This is §2.1's "the app learns" applied to the form's structure rather than to its values, and it is a display preference: it degrades to these defaults and never blocks a save (§1).

The three states are **never decided**, **opened** and **collapsed**, and the first two sentences of this section only work if the memory can tell the first from the third. M1h stored the *set of open groups*, which cannot: a diver who collapsed *Times & depth* would find it open again on the next dive, because "I closed this" and "I have never touched this" were the same absent row. **"Opens by default" is therefore an answer for the untouched group only** — an explicit collapse outranks it. Found while building M1i, from a brief that asked for a starting state the storage could not express (§10).

**Where the two halves disagree, the value wins**, and the owner's own statement of the rule is the clearest one anybody has managed: *no data = collapsed, data carried = not collapsed*. A remembered collapse cancels the default-open state and nothing more. So a collapse holds for the dive you are logging, and the next dive re-opens whichever groups carry-over has refilled — which is the rule working, not the rule failing.

That was written up once as a defect ("collapsing *Gas & cylinders* can never stick — the chevron is a lie") and it is not one; the note is kept because the reasoning error is instructive. **Four of the seven groups carry** — *Gas & cylinders*, *Water & entry*, *Equipment*, *People* — so a diver on their fifth dive meets five groups open, and that looks like the complaint that started M1h arriving from the other side. It is not the same thing. Those groups are open *because they contain that diver's data*, which is the app showing its work; the M1h complaint was about groups that were **empty and shut**.

**"I never fill this group" needs no separate answer, and this is why v1 has no "Fields I use" screen** (owner's call, M1i, dropping it from scope). A group a diver never fills holds no data, so it never carries, so it stays collapsed — it costs one header row rather than a whole group, and that is cheap enough that a settings screen for it is machinery earning nothing. The four groups that *do* carry are the apparent exception, and **the clear control already answers them**: clearing a carried field writes a real blank (§0.6, §1), so the next dive carries nothing and the group goes quiet. Clearing your buddy once is what "I do not log buddies" means, said in the form rather than in settings, and it is a gesture that already exists.

**The first dive opens everything** (owner's call, M1i). With an empty logbook nothing carries and nothing is remembered, so the value rule would leave a first-time diver with the two default-open groups and five closed ones — the form at its least informative on the one occasion nobody knows what it holds. An empty logbook therefore opens every group, on the same reasoning as the first-run screen teaching §0.1: this is the only time the whole shape is worth seeing, and the condition decays on its own, since it can never be true again.

### 2.3 Autocomplete that builds the database

**A site or centre is chosen on a page, never typed into the form** (issue #19, 2026-11-15, the owner's design). The dive form's *Site* and *Centre* rows open *Choose a site* / *Choose a centre*, and a place reaches a dive in exactly two ways: picked from that page's list, or created from it. Typing into the row — which this section described until then — failed five ways at once on the owner's first TestFlight dives: the form searched only names from the diver's own past dives, never the catalogue or the server — both of which this section's first bullet had promised it; adding a site gave no visible sign of it; a site just added could not be found; search and the duplicate check read different sources; and a known name typed in full was saved unlinked. He asked for a redesign rather than a patch. §0.6 has the pages as drawn, §10 each call.

- **One search, and the device answers first.** The list is the device's copy of the catalogue and the diver's own dives, read live — instant, fully offline, and a row written a moment ago, by the create page or by the server's answer, appears as it lands. Live search adds anything newer when online: `useCatalogueSupplement` asks `search_sites` / `search_centers` once typing settles and **writes what comes back into the device's catalogue**, which the list then re-reads. **Accents fold on both sides**, so `zelezna` finds `Železná` (M2j) — and where the two folds disagree the diver sees the **union**, because the device answers first and the server supplements rather than replaces. The raw text goes over the wire: a client that pre-folded its query would fold twice and diverge silently. A guest's search reaches no server — §5 revokes `anon` — so until #28 a guest's list holds their own places.
  **A site is found by any name it goes by** (issue #31). Its other names (`alt_names`, §6 — *Stone Gate* for *Kamena vrata*) are matched beside its name on both sides: on the device by `browseCatalogue` and `alsoCalled` (domain/search.ts), through the same fold, and on the server by `search_sites`, through `folded_names` and an index of their own (§5). A row found only by another name is listed by its own name — never among the name-prefix matches, since the name it shows is not what was typed — and says which other name found it, *also called Stone Gate* (§0.6), in the picker and the Sites directory alike: a row whose name looks nothing like what was typed otherwise reads as a wrong answer.
- **One list, grouped by *have you dived here?*** With nothing typed it is the diver's history: **Dived here** (a centre: **Dived with**) — every catalogue row a *logged* dive belongs to, by the rule the row's own page uses (the id first, then the folded name) — **the dive being edited aside**, as the form leaves it out of its own suggestions — most recently dived first — by each row's latest dive in §2.5's order, so two sites dived on one day stand as the dive numbers put them — the dives counted the logbook's way. Typing narrows that and adds **Other sites** / **Other centres** — every other row whose folded name contains the query, name-prefix matches first, **at any distance**: a diver back-filling logs at home is nowhere near the dive, so the catalogue is never cut to what is close. The centre picker adds two answers while nothing is typed: **Near [the dive's site]** — every catalogue centre within 10 km of the site the dive is paired to, when that site has a position, whatever its provenance, less those already under *Dived with* — and **No centre**, which empties the field exactly as the clear ring does. **Near me** replaces all of it with one group, **Near you · within 50 km**: every row with a position inside the radius, dived or not, nearest first, still filtered by the query. A row with no position cannot be placed on a distance list and is found by name instead.
- **Near me asks the device only when pressed, and keeps nothing.** It takes `roughPosition` (§4.1) — a fix good enough to sort a 50 km list, never `currentPosition`, whose job is a pin that is stored — and asks the same two RPCs around it (`p_latitude`, `p_longitude`, `p_radius_m`), writing their rows into the catalogue as typing's supplement does. The fix orders the list and is written nowhere: a dive's GPS row stays its own act (§2.1). Refused, the pill turns back off and says why in words for a list — never the GPS row's *"too rough to pin a dive site"*.
- **A suggestion is offered when picking it would do something**, which is not the same as when it differs from what was typed. The rule is "never offer back the value the field already holds", and **it suppresses on the raw spelling** — folded forms decide what *matches*, raw spelling decides what is *offered* (M2k). Suppressing on the fold, as it did until then, withheld exactly the offers worth making: a diver who typed `zelezna` in full was not shown `Železná`, and one who typed `blue hole` was never shown `Blue Hole`, though picking either sets the paired `site_id` (§6) and writes the catalogue's own spelling. **The server never had this suppression** — `search_sites` returns an exact match like any other — so this moved the device's rule toward the server's rather than away from it. **Since #19 it governs buddy and guide alone.** The picker withholds nothing: the place the field already holds carries a check wherever it appears, and the *Selected:* line names it whether it appears or not. Picking any row sets the name snapshot and the id together through the one path (§6), which also runs §2.1's site defaults and drops the carried mark.
- **Every name is a site** (the owner's call, 2026-11-15). There is no free text and no *"just this dive"* row: a site or centre on a dive is always a picked or created catalogue row, shared with the community whenever the diver is signed in. **This reverses what this bullet said from M2o** — that creating a site was an act offered under the row, that a dive whose name is never published keeps working, and that *"§6's snapshot is the ordinary case, not a degraded one"*. For new dives it is not a case at all any more. §6's snapshot still stands beside every id, and old dives carrying a name and no id still read as they did; they are test data, which the owner deletes. A quieter private path can be added later if a real need appears.
  **M2o's gate went with the inline offer.** *"Does this name already name a row"* decided when *Add "Kotelna" as a new site* appeared under the row. The picker offers *Create* for any typed text, because a name that already names a row is listed above *Create*, a tap away — the duplicate check is no longer the first place a diver meets an existing site. (§10 keeps M2o's lesson, which outlives the gate.)
- **Creating opens a page with the place's facts** — *New dive site* / *New dive centre*, pushed from *Create "…"* with the typed text as its name, which is the only thing required. **What a new site inherits from the dive**: its pin when it has one, and `entry`, `salinity` and `water_body` — the three §2.2 classifies as properties of the *place* rather than of the day, and the three §2.1 then prefills *from* the site. Each is marked *from this dive* on the page — the salinity under its water body since #53, not drawn for the ocean and captioned *from the water body* when the rule chose it — and the diver can change it. **A site's `entry` is a set** (#31), so the dive's one way in seeds it as its only member, and the diver adds the others. Seeding nothing would leave that prefill with nothing to prefill from on every site this app ever creates. **Not `max_depth_m`**: §6 calls that the site's own depth, and a 12 m dive on a 40 m wall would publish "this site is 12 m deep" — so since #31 the page asks for it instead, typed in the diver's units and never filled from the dive. **A centre inherits its name alone, and its position always starts empty** — the form's pin is where the diver entered the water, so writing it to a centre files a dive site as the shop's address. A centre takes an optional website instead, and since #31 an address. **Everything else #31 added is optional too**, and starts empty (§0.6 has the page): the name stays the only thing required.
  **A new place is never given the phone's position unasked.** *Use current position* asks on a press, through `currentPosition` unchanged — this position *is* stored, so M2l's 100 m rule holds — and nothing else on the page asks. Back-filling at home is why: a position taken by default would publish a dive site at the diver's house. (*Choose on map* is #30.) A site's create page also has an optional *Altitude* row (in *The site*), typed — or, on iOS when the receiver vouches for it, taken from the same press's fix by tapping the offer it brings, *Use GPS altitude · 12 m*; the press itself never writes the row (§0.6, §10).
- **The country is derived from the row's own pin and from nothing else**, so a site created out of signal has **`null`** and stays correct: the pin is evidence, the country is a derivation anyone can redo later, and a guess is neither. It is stored as **ISO 3166-1 alpha-2**, not a localized name, which would otherwise file one country under as many spellings as there are device locales (M2o). The create page shows it read-only, as the code, and `—` with no position. **The one exception is a dive centre with an address and no pin** (issue #31; §10 has why): it takes the country of its address, shown as `HR · from the address`. `countryForAddress` (platform/geocode.ts) looks the address up and takes the **first** result's country — the geocoder's best guess for a vague address — within the one `GEOCODE_TIMEOUT_MS` a pin's country gets. **The address gives the country and never a pin**; a pin's country wins whenever there is a pin; it needs signal, as a pin's does; and it runs on Android only when the location permission is already granted, and never asks for it.
- **The duplicate check runs when a site is saved** — on the create page's *Save*, where it moved from under the form's row (#19); a centre saves straight away, since §5 declines `similar_centers`. A fuzzy check suggests near-matches: *"Did you mean Shark Point?"*, standing above the button. Each shows its country and how far it is, and a tap opens the existing site's own page, whose *Use this site* picks it instead and returns to the form (owner's call, PR #35: a diver checks it really is the same place first) — through the same pairing a list pick uses (§6), never a second path. **The server's matches are written to the device's catalogue before they are offered** (as live search writes its answers), and one the phone still does not hold is not offered: a dive paired with a site the phone has no row for would show no country, get no site defaults and be missing from *Dived here* until some later sync. A near-match is **a suggestion and never a refusal**: two real sites can carry similar names and the diver is standing at one of them, so the button re-words to *"Save as a new site anyway"* and the second press commits. The row is then written on the device — dirty, a client UUIDv7, the country awaited before the insert — picked into the form as a list pick is, and both pages close. A write that fails says so on the page and changes nothing on the dive.
  **The check runs offline too, and it is a different check** (M2p). The server's is `similar_sites`' trigram; the device's is **exact equality of the folded whole name** against its own catalogue copy — which catches `kotelna` against `Kotelna` and `Divoka Sarka` against `Divoká Šárka`, the duplicate §5 actually names, for the price of a read it was already able to do. It deliberately misses `Sharks Point` against `Shark Point`, because approximating `pg_trgm` in JavaScript would be **a second implementation of what a duplicate is**, free to drift from `name_match_floor()` where nobody would see it (§4.1). Substring matching is the tempting middle and is wrong: it makes `Kotelna` a duplicate of `Kotelna II`.
  **An exact match and a near-match are different findings and get different sentences.** *"Did you mean X?"* is a guess, and the device's match is not one — the name is the same name. Asking it as a question would put uncertainty into the one case that has none. **The create page is the first screen to draw the second sentence** — *"“Kotelna” is already a site."* The form asked *Did you mean* about an identical name too, and its own docblock reported the gap for this section rather than rewording it (§10).
  **Since #31 the check runs both ways, and a third finding has a sentence of its own.** A new site's **name** is checked against existing names *and* existing other names, and its **other names** against existing names — otherwise *Stone Gate*, created with the other name *Kamena vrata*, slips past an existing *Kamena vrata*. The server's `similar_sites` takes the other names Save will write (`p_alt_names`) beside the name, and the device's exact fold asks the same two questions (`nearMatches`). **Other name against other name is not compared**: the issue names these two directions and no third, and two places that share a nickname are not thereby one place. What a match *is*, the device reads off the row it holds (`twinRelation`, domain/suggest.ts): the same name — *“Kotelna” is already a site.* — another name for it — *“Kamena vrata” is another name for Stone Gate.*, which is neither a guess nor the same name — or a guess, *Did you mean …?*. A fuzzy server match on another name is a guess, so it reads *Did you mean* — and **says which other name it came through** (the owner's call, 2026-11-17): *Kamena vrat* typed reads *Did you mean “Stone Gate” (also called Kamena vrata)?*. Only the server can say it, since only it scored the names, so `similar_sites` hands back each row with the other name it matched through (`matched_name`, from `best_alt_name`) when that was its best score, and nothing when the site's own name was; a guess through the site's own name reads *Did you mean “Stone Gate”?* as before (§10).
  **A site created out of signal is created, and says nothing about it.** The check that could not run has no gesture to offer, and a sentence explaining its absence would fire on every out-of-signal site for ever, under the most-used control in the app.
- **Guests create too** (the owner's call, 2026-11-15), **reversing "only a signed-in diver is offered it"**, which this section said from M2o. A guest's place is written on the phone exactly as a signed-in diver's created out of signal is, and is published when they sign in (§5). The create page says so in one line and the *Create* row in its meta; nothing else prompts.
- Buddies and guides autocomplete from your own past entries only — they stay private text, not user accounts, and their suggestions stay under the field (§0.6).

### 2.4 Prepare before, finish after

Logs can be half-written in advance. On the boat, set up the coming dives — date, site, cylinder, starting pressure — and they wait at the top of the list as *Up next*, excluded from stats and dive numbering. After surfacing, *Complete dive* asks only for the missing numbers: duration, depths, ending pressure. A planned dive has no dive number until it's completed.

### 2.5 Dive numbering

**Dive numbers are computed, never stored:** chronological position plus your pre-Ponor dive count (`dives_before`, asked once at onboarding, editable in settings any time). Backfilling an old dive slots it into place and renumbers everything after it automatically — on every device, with zero sync churn. Same-day dives order by time in. When times are missing the diver can order them by hand, which is what `manual_order` stores — a nullable integer used only as a tie-break within one date, and **reordering rewrites the whole day as 1..n** rather than writing a position onto the dragged dive alone (see §10). A dive that has been ordered by hand sorts before one that has not, on the same reasoning as timed-before-untimed; dives with neither fall back to creation order, which is deterministic across devices and is almost always already correct. No per-dive number override in v1 (layer it on later only if real users ask).

## 3. Screens

Four tabs plus a full-screen dive form. Onboarding is two steps — pick units and, for switchers, how many dives you already have; optionally sign in — then straight to "Log your first dive". **Onboarding is not built; the "optionally sign in" step is the empty logbook's account card** (issue #24, §0.6), which offers the account under *Log your first dive* without standing in front of it.

- **Dives** — auto-grouped into trips (same dive centre, gaps up to 3 days — §10); planned dives pinned on top as "Up next" **with their date**; search; row = number, site, metadata, and the depth value as the row's anchor (§0.6); hand-ordering behind a day strip; logging a dive is the screen's primary action — a bottom-right "+" while Ponor is tab-less, moving into the top-right capsule when the tab bar lands (see below).

  **A dive with a recorded series grows §0.4's two drawings, and neither belongs to this screen alone** (M3p). In a row it is one muted monochrome path beside the metadata; six call sites render that row and all six feed it from `useDiveSampleShapes`, so a shape appears wherever a dive does rather than only where it was first wanted. On the **dive detail** the same series becomes a card between the hero and the first cluster: the depth curve stroked band by band through the depth gradient, the deepest *sample* marked in that sample's own colour, gridlines and labels every 10 m, the muted gas trace on its own unlabelled scale, and a fixed readout above the plot that a finger scrubs along the curve — a **horizontal** drag scrubs and a vertical one still scrolls the page, or the card would trap the scroll of the `ScrollView` it sits in, which reads as a broken screen rather than as a feature. Beneath the card, and deliberately not part of it, sits an **`Imported from`** row naming the computer the dive was read off (`dives.import_device`), falling back to the importer's own label where the export named no model — a computer can export a dive with no profile in it, so the provenance is a fact about the dive rather than a caption under a drawing. It carries **no chevron**, per §0.6, because there is nowhere to navigate to. None of this appears for a dive logged by hand, which is §0.4 rather than a special case.
- **Map** — your dives grouped **by site identity, not by geometry** (badge = count per site); tapping a site shows your dives there with a depth/temp summary; a toggle between your dives, community sites and **community centres**, one layer at a time.
  **The three layers are a filter, not a mode** (owner's call, M3e, reversing M3c). Any combination shows at once — dives alone, or dives with the sites and centres around them — because the question a diver actually has on a map is *what is near here*, and a mode can only ever answer one third of it at a time.

  **One disc, three interiors** (M3e): a dive keeps its numeral, a community site is a plain dot, a centre carries a storefront glyph. **The shape never varies, which turns M3c's failure into the mechanism** — two overlapping discs are one disc, so the thing that made mixed shapes unreadable is what makes mixed kinds readable.

  **That much holds. The interiors do not, and the owner found it by using the map** (M3k): *"map markers are all same/similar and user have no idea which one is dive log, site or center."* M3e checked it by looking and recorded that the three read as three instantly; a builder who knows what each mark means is the worst possible judge of whether it is legible, and this is the third time this session a *looked-at* claim has been overturned by the owner *using* it.
  **Two things were wrong.** A community site is distinguished by **having no interior at all** — an absence is not a mark, and it is the weakest signal available. And §0.6's *"a symbol that needs a legend has failed"* was answered by putting the legend **one press away in the filter control**, which is a legend by any honest reading: the rule was satisfied on a technicality rather than met.
  **What a replacement has to do:** separate three kinds with no colour (§0.1) and **no legend at all**, at 26 pt, over cartography. Ink weight is the axis that has not been tried — filled against outlined is visible before anything inside it is — and the interior can then carry the second distinction rather than all three.

  **A dive and the site it was at are the same pin, and that is an identity rather than a coincidence.** §2.3 copies the dive's pin into the site it creates *and* pairs them by id — so exact overlap **is** the paired case, and dropping a catalogue row that already stands under one of the diver's own marks removes precisely it. The place's sheet still carries the row's facts, so nothing is lost with the dot. **Never matched by folded name**: that would take Egypt's Blue Hole off the map for a diver who has been to Croatia's.

  **M3c chose modes after building both and looking, and its measurements stand as the brief for whoever draws the marks** — they are what does not work, now that simultaneity is a requirement rather than an option. A square beside a circle reads as **the same mark drawn wrong**; two overlapping squares read as one stacked card; and the layer's own summary could not describe a mixed population (`Community · 3 sites` over one site and two centres). What was a reason not to mix them is now the constraint on mixing them: **§0.1 leaves no colour to separate the three kinds, so the distinction has to be carried by shape, ink weight or an inner glyph — and plain shape has already been tried and failed.**

  **Switching a layer on moves the camera when what arrived is not in view** (M3l). Turning sites on used to leave the header reading `2 sites` over a map showing none — `MapView` reads its region **once**, so a filter switched on after mount could only add marks the camera had no opinion about, off-frame and unannounced. That is the *first* experience of the community layer rather than an edge case, since a catalogue arrives by sync and sits wherever the diver has never been.
  **Three halves of the rule, each of which could have been got wrong.** Not on every switch — a diver who can already see some of what they turned on keeps the camera they placed. Not only when the map is empty — it was full of their own dive throughout. And the refit frames **everything drawn**, not the arrival: framing the arrival alone would carry the diver away from their own dives to look at somebody else's sites. Nothing refits for a layer switched **off**, or one that arrives with no pins — §1's sentences already own that state.

  **A map can be pointed at one place** (issue #21). *Show on map* — on a dive, a site's page or a centre's page — pushes the same screen over the page with a `focus`: it opens on the place at the zoom a single mark gets (about 2.2 km), its kind of mark switched on, its mark selected, and for a place of the diver's own its sheet already up, with the place moved into the top half so the sheet does not cover it. `‹ Back` returns to the page, and a *Directions* pill hands the place to the device's maps, which is what the card did before. **A site is whichever mark stands there** — the diver's own place where they have dived it (the catalogue's dot is not drawn under it, above), the community's dot where they have not — and `domain/mapFocus.ts` decides which. Offline it behaves as the tab does: the place and its mark come from the device's tables, and only the tiles need a network. §10 has why it is pushed rather than the tab switched to.

  **Named cost, narrowed by #19:** a centre is missing from the map unless someone gave it a position. §2.3 still gives a new centre nothing of the dive's but its name; since #19 its create page can take the phone's position on a press, and that is the only way one gets a pin. Grouping by identity rather than by pixel distance is what makes the badge mean *"your dives here"* rather than *"pins that happen to overlap at this zoom"*, and it needs no clustering library (M2n).
- **Stats** — total dives, hours underwater, deepest dive (**`domain/logbookStats.ts` already owns these three**, since the Dives header shows them — M3 renders them, it does not recompute them); countries and sites visited; RMV trend; currency (days since your last dive, refresher nudge after 6 months); charts later, counters first.
  **RMV carries a sparkline: one bar per dive, drawn from `View`s, no dependency** (M3d, the owner asked for a chart). §3's "counters first" is not only taste — this app had no drawing primitive when that was written, `react-native-svg` was deliberately absent, and a bar row is what the first-run legend and the rating dots are already made of. Three rules make it a figure rather than a decoration. **The window is exactly the dives the figure averages**, so the shape and the caption read one array and cannot disagree — ten bars under a sentence naming five is a row arguing with itself. **A dive with no RMV never enters the series**: not a bar, not a gap, because a zero-height bar says *you used no gas* (§0.4's instinct, applied to a different figure), and fewer than two values draws nothing, since one bar normalised against itself is always full height. **And the bars are measured from zero rather than min-to-max**, or 14.8 against 14.9 draws as a cliff beside a Trend row reading *steady*.
  **The dependency has since arrived, and this row is still `View`s** (M3p, and §10 carries the amendment). A depth profile reopened the absence with new information; this row did not, because it genuinely is a bar row and the paragraph above still describes it word for word. Converting it would be change for symmetry's sake.

  **A figure with nothing behind it draws its row and shows an em dash** (M3a) — this screen has one subject and a **fixed inventory**, unlike the dive detail, whose rows vary with what a diver recorded, so a missing row here would read as a screen that failed rather than a figure not yet earned. **Countries shows a dash and a sentence rather than `0`**, because a nought is a claim a diver could disprove by remembering where they have been: the app does not know their countries, which is not the same as their having none.
  **No row carries §0.6's computed `=`.** That mark separates what the app derived from what the diver **entered**, and nothing on this screen was entered — a screen where every value is derived has no distinction left for the mark to draw.
- **Settings** — units (m/ft, bar/psi, °C/°F, kg/lb), language; cylinder presets; **location access**; certification wallet (agency, level, card number); account & sync, data export (CSV + JSON), **data import**, **start over**, delete account. **A guest's Settings opens with the account card** above units (issue #24), and the account & sync row steps aside while it shows.
  **`DATA IMPORT` is a section of its own, directly below `DATA EXPORT`** (M3o). They are twins — one takes the logbook out, the other brings dives in — and naming the section for the act rather than for Suunto leaves room for §9's remaining formats without renaming anything the day one lands. It is a section rather than a loose row for M3n's own reason: a row with no heading above it reads as a further fact about whatever precedes it, which is how *Location access* came to read as a third thing about cylinder presets.
  **The destructive acts live here rather than on the account screen**, which reverses a note saying Settings deliberately says nothing about who is signed in (M3j). That note was about a **display** row — telling a diver their own email back — and these are *controls that cannot exist without an account*, which is a different thing; §7.5's pending indicator, shown only when signed in, is the precedent. The placement is also what lets *"take your data first"* be a working link to the export a few rows above rather than a second copy of that flow.
  **Pressing the row opens the system Settings app at its root, not at Ponor's page** — iOS' own behaviour, not something the app chooses, so the row's text says to find Ponor there rather than pretending it lands anywhere else (M2m).

  **The location row exists because iOS asks once ever** (owner's call, M2). A diver who declines the permission at the moment §2.3's *"use my location"* first needs it gets nothing on every later tap, and no app-side prompt can reopen it — so the row states the current status and takes them to the system Settings app, which is the only place the answer can change. Reading the status must not request it: those are two operations and a settings row that prompts merely by being looked at is a worse offence than the dead tap it fixes. ("Fields I use" was listed here until M1i dropped it — §2.2.)
> **Tabs go to the bottom; search and `+` move to a top-right capsule** (owner's call, recorded during M1d, to be built with Settings). Measured on iOS 26, not recalled: Messages (no tab bar) floats a search capsule at the bottom with compose top-right; Photos (tab bar) puts its `[Library | Collections]` capsule bottom-left and search as a detached circle bottom-right. Both are a capsule left, a circle right — exactly the shape Ponor already uses for search and `+` — so the strip holds two objects and we will want three. Calendar answers it: one top-right glass capsule carrying view-toggle, magnifier and `+` as equal monochrome glyphs, with the bottom left to navigation. Ponor follows that. Rejected: keeping `+` bottom-right and hiding search behind a pull-down — more code, and a hidden affordance, which is the same complaint that already cost the reorder control a redesign. **Built with `expo-router`'s native tabs** (`unstable-native-tabs`), so iOS 26 draws its own Liquid Glass bar with the platform's scroll-edge behaviour and accessibility rather than an approximation of them — the owner's call, and the reason the bar looks right without us styling it. **The browser runs a different navigator on purpose:** expo-router's web implementation of native tabs is a *top-centre text pill* with no icons, fixed by its own CSS at `top: 24px`, which both contradicts this decision and collided with the capsule at narrow widths. Web therefore uses the ordinary JS `Tabs` through a `_layout.web.tsx`, styled to this spec. Fighting the native-tabs web CSS with selectors on its internal markup was rejected: it is an `unstable_` API and that breaks silently on upgrade. **Issue #2 kept it native** after prototyping a bar drawn to the owner's design canvas; the bar now carries the canvas's glyphs (§0.6, §10).
>
> The cost is real and accepted: the `+` leaves the thumb zone §0.5 reserves for the primary action, and stops being big. It is not abandoned — **the empty logbook is a first-run screen (§0.6) and keeps a full-size "Log your first dive" at the bottom of it**, so the diver who most needs reach keeps it, and the top-right capsule serves the returning diver with a populated list. Whether `+` is an equal glyph or carries some emphasis is decided when it is built; §10's "no accent on the `+`" binds either way, since that was about hue, not weight.

- **Tablets** — same app with adaptive layouts: list + detail side by side, map next to the list on wide screens. No separate codebase.

## 4. App stack

| Role | Choice | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Framework | Expo · React Native · TypeScript · `expo-router` | One codebase for iOS, Android, tablets; free tooling; OTA JS updates |
| Local database | `expo-sqlite` + Drizzle ORM | Typed schema, migrations, reactive `useLiveQuery`; the device is the source of truth |
| Forms | `react-hook-form` + Zod | Forms are the heart of the app; validation shared with sync payloads |
| UI | `StyleSheet` driven by the §0.2 tokens · small custom kit · `@react-native-community/datetimepicker` · `SectionList` | No styling framework: NativeWind was tried and removed in M0 (§10). Native date/time pickers (M1d — `@gorhom/bottom-sheet` was never installed; the platform picker turned out to be what "an invalid date cannot be entered" needed, and a sheet buys nothing over it); `SectionList` over FlashList for sections and section headers without an extra New-Architecture dependency (§10) — **not** for sticky headers, which M1k switched off |
| Maps | `react-native-maps` | Apple Maps on iOS, Google Maps on Android — both free on mobile. **No `supercluster`**: §3 groups by site identity, which needs no geometry (M2n) |
| State | Zustand (UI) · TanStack Query (remote search) | Persistent state lives in SQLite, not in a JS store |
| i18n | `i18next` + `expo-localization` | English + Czech from day one |
| Cloud client | `@supabase/supabase-js` + `expo-secure-store` | Auth session in the keychain, not AsyncStorage |
| Crash reporting | `@sentry/react-native` | Free developer tier; familiar tooling |
| Builds & CI | EAS Build + GitHub Actions | Free tier quota plus unlimited local builds |
| Web app — **a testing target from M1, a product after release** (§9) | `react-native-web` via Expo · `wa-sqlite` in OPFS · MapLibre | Same codebase in the browser. It already runs and logs real dives, which is the point: the blocker was the data layer M2's sync builds on, so finding it out now was cheap. Maps swap to MapLibre + OSM tiles when the Map tab lands; free static hosting, but only somewhere that sets COOP/COEP (§9) |

**Development builds, not Expo Go:** maps and Sign in with Apple are native modules, so we build with `expo-dev-client` from the start. Slightly more setup in M0, no surprises later.

**The web app** comes from the same codebase — Expo Router already targets the browser. **It is built and working now**, as a testing target rather than a supported platform; §9 records why it was pulled forward and what the data layer learned, and that is the authoritative account. What still waits for after the store release is the web app as a *product*: hosted, linked and promised, with the admin area inside it.

Two platform splits were expected here and one of them turned out backwards, which is worth keeping. Maps split as predicted — MapLibre with free OSM tiles instead of `react-native-maps`, still owed when the Map tab lands. **Storage did not:** this paragraph used to say the browser would start "in online mode against Supabase, a local cache can follow", and the opposite is what shipped — real `wa-sqlite` in OPFS, offline-first exactly as the device is, with no Supabase involved at all. A prediction recorded and then falsified is more useful than a prediction quietly deleted, so it stays.

### 4.1 One owner per rule

This project's defining defect is one rule written in two places, then drifting. It has produced dive numbers reading #2, #1, #3; a date contract asserted three ways at three strictnesses and enforced in none; a site name that said "Unnamed site" in the list and nothing at all on the detail; a cylinder material rendered "Steel" on one screen and "steel" on the next; and, twice, a *Complete dive* button wired to the plain edit route, completing nothing while saying it did. Every one shipped green.

**Each of these owns its class of rule. A second implementation is a defect, not a style preference:**

| Owner | Owns |
|---|---|
| `domain/types.ts` | Every closed vocabulary a form offers as a fixed list — the list is the source and the type is derived from it, never written twice — and **how many cylinders a rig is** (`single` 1, `twinset` 2, `sidemount` 2), which `derived.ts` calls rather than re-deriving. |
| `domain/datetime.ts` | Every reading of a `YYYY-MM-DD` or `HH:MM` string, and every conversion to or from a `Date`. Never `toISOString()` (UTC) and never `new Date('2026-08-31')` (UTC midnight) outside it — three separate bugs came from exactly those. |
| `format/display.ts` | Every conversion of a stored value into diver-facing text. |
| `format/units.ts` | The unit system, the four pairs, their conversion factors and their per-pair precision. |
| `theme/styles.ts` | Every place a token meets a style property. `theme/depth.ts` is the only reader of the depth scale; `theme/resolve.ts` the only reader of the token sets. |
| `domain/diveNumber.ts` | Dive ordering and numbering (§2.5). |
| `domain/trips.ts` | Trip grouping (§3). |
| `db/dives.ts` | Every write to a dive, and the `undefined` = don't touch / `null` = clear patch contract. |
| `db/integers.ts` | Which columns are INTEGER — read off the schema (`integerColumns`), so a new one is covered the day it exists — and how a write stores one: rounded, and a value that is not a number at all as null (`storedInteger`). Moved out of `db/dives.ts` by issue #31, when a site's `wreck_sunk_year` made `db/catalogue.ts` the second writer with an INTEGER column (§10). |
| `db/gearPresets.ts` | Every write to a preset, under the same contract — including that a write changing nothing must not advance `updated_at`, which §7's last-write-wins is keyed on. |
| `db/liveQuery.ts` | Two adjacent facts about a live read, and both are about *time*, not content: whether it has produced an answer yet (`resolved` — because "still looking" and "nothing there" are what an empty array cannot tell apart), and whether a failure is still what the read last said (because `useLiveQuery` sets `error` in its failure paths and **never clears it**, so a notice keyed on it outlives the failure it reports). |
| `db/tombstone.ts` | `liveRows(table)` — the tombstone filter every read of every soft-deleted table applies. Was `liveDives` alone until a second such table existed (M1e). |
| `domain/catalogueDives.ts` | Whether a dive belongs to a catalogue row — the id tier, the folded-name tier, and §2.4's filter. One rule over two tables (M3f); `siteDives.ts` and `centerDives.ts` keep only the pair of columns each asks about, which is the part that could never be shared. |
| `domain/centerDives.ts` | Which dives were with one centre. **Deliberately not `siteIdentity.ts`'s question** — that one groups dives by the place they share, this one matches dives to a catalogue row the diver has open, and written as a shared key it would answer the wrong one (M3c). |
| `platform/openWebsite.ts` | Handing a URL to the platform, and what happens when it will not take it. |
| `platform/openContact.ts` | What a centre's phone number and email may make another diver's phone do (issue #31) — `openWebsite.ts`' argument one module over, since both are community text and `openURL` is a general door into the device. `telUrl` and `mailtoUrl` build a `tel:` or `mailto:` address only from text that *is* a number or one plain address — the email by an **allowlist** (§10) — so nothing built from text that failed ever reaches `openURL`. A centre's address goes to the maps app as a search, through `mapsSearchUrl` (platform/mapsLink.ts). |
| `domain/certifications.ts` | What a certification means — including that an expiry is a comparison of calendar dates, and that a card expiring today has not expired. |
| `domain/logbook.ts` | What the app says when it cannot open the logbook. One sentence, four screens; they had already drifted into two spellings of the apostrophe before this had an owner (M3b). |
| `domain/siteIdentity.ts` | Which dives happened at one site — the id first, then the folded name. Extracted when the Stats tab needed the answer the Map was already computing (M3a); the map keeps a third tier of its own on top. |
| `domain/siteDefaults.ts` | §2.1's precedence — diver over site over carry-over — and what a `null` column means. |
| `domain/merges.ts` | Where a merged catalogue row sends a dive — including a chain of merges, and the termination that a cycle cannot outlive. |
| `platform/geocode.ts` | Turning a pin into a country, and the rule that a country is derived from a pin or is nothing — **and the rule's one exception, which it owns too**: a centre's address, when it has no pin (`countryForAddress`, issue #31; §2.3 states it, §10 has why). |
| `platform/withTimeout.ts` | How long the app waits on the outside world before giving up on it. Extracted when a second caller arrived (M2o); since issue #19 it also bounds the server's half of §2.3's duplicate check. |
| `hooks/useForegroundReturn.ts` | What counts as **coming back to the app** — a transition into `active`, not every `AppState` event. Two callers now (§7.5's sync triggers and §3's location row), which is why it was extracted from inside the first rather than written a second time (M2m). |
| `platform/locationPermission.ts` | Whether this app may locate the diver — the five states it can be in, and the split that keeps **reading** the status from **asking** for it. Four controls read it, and three of them may prompt: the dive form's GPS row, and since issue #19 the create page's *Use current position* and the picker's *Near me*. §3's Settings row reads and never asks. |
| `platform/location.ts` | How a fix is taken, as **two requests for two questions** (issue #19). `currentPosition` takes a pin that will be stored: the accuracy asked for, the timeout waited, and **how rough a fix may be and still be stored**. `roughPosition` takes a position to sort *Near me*'s 50 km list by and stores nothing — a last-known fix up to ten minutes old, else a balanced one within ten seconds, at any accuracy. **A second owner for a different question, not a copy**: the two disagree on every parameter and on what a refusal is, and share the one function that reads and asks for the permission. |
| `format/positionRefusal.ts` | What each way of failing to get a **stored** pin says — one sentence per refusal, keyed by `POSITION_REFUSALS` so a new refusal cannot arrive without one. Its two callers are `currentPosition`'s: the dive form's GPS row and, since issue #19, the create page's *Use current position*. A module rather than a part of `display.ts`, which imports nothing from `platform/`. *Near me* words its own refusals for a list and does not use it. |
| `supabase/migrations/` | The Postgres shape of every synced table — columns, types, defaults, indexes and RLS. Also **`public.name_fold`**: how a name is read before it is matched on the server, and the expression the trigram indexes are built over. Its client twin is `domain/search.ts`'s `foldForMatching`, and the two **cannot** be one function — one is JavaScript, one is a Postgres dictionary. This is the table's first entry with a known, documented divergence, pinned by name in `search.test.ts` rather than papered over. |
| `src/db/schemaParity.test.ts` | The tie between that and `src/db/schema.ts`. §6 has claimed "the same schema lives in SQLite and Postgres" since M0 and nothing checked it; a column added to one and not the other is a row that syncs to nowhere and fails no gate. Legitimate differences are an explicit exception list, so each one costs a deliberate edit. |
| `domain/logbookStats.ts` | What a logbook adds up to — total dives, hours underwater, deepest dive, in SI and unformatted. Two callers by construction (§0.6's header line and M3's Stats screen), which is why it is a module and not a `useMemo`: two independent counts of "how many dives have I done" that disagree by one planned dive is this table's defining defect. |
| `db/settings.ts` | What a settings row *means* — every coercion of a stored string back into a value, and every default a missing or nonsense row falls back to. Includes the form-group memory's three states (§2.2) and its upgrade from M1h's open-ids array, which is a stored-shape migration and therefore has exactly one home. A second key/value path is the defect this table exists to name. |
| `domain/presets.ts` | Preset ordering, preset name identity, and **what refuses a preset save** — the three sentences and the verdict behind them, shared by the dive form's capture and §3's editor. Two of the three were byte-identical copies on the two screens until M1e's fix round; §4.1's i18next exception below covers duplicated *field labels*, not a rule's verdict. |
| `domain/suggest.ts` | What a field offers a diver about a value they are typing: what autocomplete suggests and in what order, and — since M2p — whether that value already names a catalogue row, which is the device half of §2.3's duplicate check. One module because they are one question asked of one string; **split it if a third kind of answer arrives**, rather than widening this sentence again. Since issue #19 its suggestions serve buddy and guide alone. What a set of dives adds up to is counted once, by `historyOf` — `suggestionHistory` hands it the dives under a typed name, and `domain/placePicker.ts` the dives paired to a picker row. |
| `domain/placePicker.ts` | What the site and centre picker lists, under which heading, in what order, and which row is checked — *Dived here* / *Dived with*, *Other*, *Near you*, *Near [site]*, and the two radii (issue #19). Asks `divesAtSite` / `divesWithCenter` which dives belong to a row, so the picker shows the history the row's own page shows. The screen words the list and decides nothing about it. |
| `domain/geo.ts` | The distance between two points (haversine, km), for the picker's two Near lists; `formatDistance` (format/display.ts) words it. |
| `domain/search.ts` | `foldForMatching` — how text is read before it is compared, on **both** sides and for both features. §10 puts diacritic folding in M2 for search and autocomplete alike, so written twice that change lands in one place and leaves the other behind. |
| `domain/mapFocus.ts` | Which mark a place *is* when a map is pointed at it — a dive's place, a centre's own mark, and for a site whichever of the diver's place or the community's dot is actually drawn — and where such a map opens (issue #21). Asks `groupDivesByPlace` and `catalogueSiteIdentity` rather than restating them, so it cannot select a mark the map has taken away. |
| `navigation/mapFocusLink.ts` | The three routes to a map pointed at a place (`/dive/[id]/map`, `/site/[id]/map`, `/center/[id]/map`), and where its Back lands from a cold link. `platform/showOnMap.ts` is the one caller that pushes them, and its `.web` twin opens OpenStreetMap instead. |
| `navigation/placePick.ts` | Handing a pick back to the dive form — the one place a pushed route returns a value (issue #19). The form registers a request under a token and pushes the picker with it; the picker, the create page two screens up, or a near-match's site page three up (*Use this site*), completes it once and closes what was pushed for it, and closes them even for a token a reload has lost. A token completed or forgotten is settled: completing it again does nothing, so no second completion can close the form. |
| `navigation/leaveScreen.ts` | Where leaving a screen lands, and the guard that a diver who deep-linked into it still reaches somewhere real. Two destinations exist now — the dive list and Settings — so the rule is shared rather than per-screen. |
| `cloud/supabase.ts` | Whether there is a backend at all, and the client when there is one. Reads the two `EXPO_PUBLIC_*` variables as literal member expressions — see §10, that is load-bearing, not style — and treats absent, blank and whitespace alike as *no backend configured*. |
| `cloud/sessionStore.ts` | Where an auth session is kept: the keychain on a device (`expo-secure-store`, §4), `localStorage` in a browser, which is **not** a keychain and is acceptable only under §9's testing-target rule. |
| `cloud/addToCatalogue.ts` | Every write of a new site or centre, and §2.3's duplicate check (`findSiteTwins` — the device's exact fold and `similar_sites`, asked together, never throwing, and the server's half given four seconds before the device's answer stands alone; the server's matching rows are written to the device before they are offered, and each match carries the phone's row's country and position). Moved out of the dive form when the create pages needed them without one (issue #19); what a new row may hold is still `siteFactsFrom`'s, `platform/geocode.ts`'s and `db/catalogue.ts`'s. |
| `src/testing/` | Shared test guards, so a guard cannot be broken in one copy and sound in four others. |
| `src/platform/` | Behaviour that genuinely differs by platform, so a screen never branches on platform itself. |
| `components/TokenChips.tsx` | A fixed-choice field that holds a **set** — a site's types, a centre's services and agencies (issue #31). `OptionChips`' look, from the same style keys, so a chosen chip is the inverted one in both; but a press toggles one member, a screen reader hears each chip as a checkbox, and a token this build does not offer is kept rather than dropped by the next press (§10's store-and-flag). A second component rather than a mode on `OptionChips`, whose docblock already turned down a second behaviour inside the one component its callers share. |
| `components/ChoiceGrid.tsx` | How an option of `OptionChips` and `TokenChips` looks and is laid out, as chips or as tiles (issue #6) — the styles, the ink and ground handed to a drawn icon, the tile grid and its spacers. One owner because what makes an option read as chosen is a rule (§4.1); each caller keeps only what its press does and the state a screen reader hears. |
| `components/symbolName.ts` | The per-platform key an SF/Material symbol is requested by — **owner enforced and every caller witnessed** (M1h): every symbol the app ships has its Android and web names asserted, not only the iOS one that a simulator would have shown, and a census of the source holds the test to that rather than a list (#49). The two tab layouts are handed **pre-resolved** icons by `navigation/tabs.ts`, so passing an unresolved symbol there does not compile — which is how a rule reaches `src/app/`, a tree that carries no tests by design. |

**Three rules keep it that way.**

*Derive, or tie at compile time.* A list that can be computed from another is computed — `FRESH_FIELDS` is the schema's keys minus `CARRIED_FIELDS`, not a second list. One that cannot be gets a type-level assertion that fails the build when the two diverge; `TankFormFieldsMatchTank` and `StatusFormValuesMatchDive` in `diveFormSchema.ts` are the pattern to copy. Adding a member to a hand-maintained option list used to produce a save-blocking rejection and a missing chip, silently.

*One deliberate exception, until i18next.* Roughly twenty-five field labels are duplicated as literals across the form and the dive detail — "Water temp", "Max depth", and so on. They agree today, and each is one edit from becoming the `O2 %` / `O₂` drift again. They are **not** unified yet on purpose: translation has to key every one of them, and that pass is where the set belongs. Doing it twice would be waste; leaving it unrecorded would make it look like an oversight.

*A deliberate near-duplicate names its siblings.* Some rules look alike and answer different questions, and unifying them is itself a bug. Three read a dive's place: `tripKeyOf` groups (centre first, **may be null**, so "no place recorded" stays distinguishable from a dive actually named "Unnamed site"), `diveSiteLabel` displays (site first, **never null**, because a row with no heading is a blank line), and `ReorderControls`'s `rowLabel` speaks (**omits** the placeholder, because position already identifies the row and "Unnamed site" would be noise in speech). Each carries a note naming the others and the question each answers. Without that note, the next reader sees three copies of one rule and helpfully unifies them.

## 5. Backend on Supabase

One Supabase project (EU region, Frankfurt) provides everything server-side; we write SQL and policies, no server code and no hosting. Row-Level Security is the security model: dives are readable and writable only by their owner; sites and centers are readable by **every signed-in user**.

**"Readable by everyone" was ambiguous and M2a had to pick.** It can mean every *user* or every *caller* — and the second makes the whole catalogue public to anyone holding the publishable key, which ships inside the app. The schema revokes `anon` on all six tables, on the reasoning that opening this later is two documented statements and closing it after a scrape is impossible. Revisit when the community catalogue is worth showing off; the app itself never needs it, since §2.3's autocomplete runs off the on-device copy.

- **Auth:** email and password, the only method in v1; Sign in with Apple and Google come together in an update after release (#13; the owner's call, 2026-11-19, in #22). Apple is mandatory once Google is offered (App Store rule 4.8).
  **The email method was a one-time code, then a magic link, and is now a password** (owner's call, M2). Codes read better on mobile and were chosen for that; delivering one needs an email template edit, **template edits need custom SMTP, and custom SMTP needs Pro** ($25/mo, which §8 puts thousands of users away). The fallback was the default magic link — free, one redirect entry — and the owner rejected it on taste, which is a legitimate reason for the one screen every diver meets. **Email and password needs no email at all**: sign-up with confirmation disabled sends nothing, so it clears the same wall from the other side.
  **Password reset works, and the paragraph that said it did not was wrong.** The wall is on *editing* a template, not on *sending* one: Supabase ships default `Confirm signup` and `Reset password` templates and the built-in mailer sends them on the free tier. Recovery therefore needs a `ponor://` redirect entry and deep-link handling, and nothing else. **Recovery is a link and login is not**, which is the distinction that makes this sit alongside the owner's objection rather than against it — a link is a bad thing to meet every time you open an app and a fine thing to meet once, on the day you have forgotten something.

  **Recovery is built, and the confirmation link now signs you in** (2026-11-14; the owner: "the login/signup experience is not good"). *Forgot password?* sits under the password row in sign-in mode and sends Supabase's default `Reset password` mail; the link in it — and the link in the confirmation mail — opens the app at `ponor://`, the **one** redirect both mails use, so the project's allowed-redirect list needs nothing beyond the entry confirmation already depends on. `cloud/AuthLinks.tsx`, mounted at the root, takes the link to the account screen and exchanges it for a session (`completeAuthLink`, cloud/auth.ts): a confirmation link signs the diver in and adopts this phone's dives exactly as a password sign-in does (§7.4), and a reset link does the same and then asks for a new password, marked as *new* so the platform can suggest a strong one. The two are told apart by what the exchange reports (`redirectType: 'recovery'`), not by where they point.

  **The client runs PKCE for this, and that is the security argument.** A link signs in only with the verifier the phone stored when it asked for the mail, so a link made up by someone else cannot sign this phone into *their* account — which the default implicit flow, tokens in a fragment, would let any link do. The cost is that a mail link works on the phone that asked for it: opened anywhere else it still confirms the address, and the screen says to sign in with the password. Both sentences on screen say "open it on this phone" for that reason. **Unverified end to end**: no real mail has been sent from this tree (it has no credentials for the project), so the first real reset — and the first confirmation since PKCE — is the test, and the Supabase dashboard's *Redirect URLs* must still list `ponor://`.

  **Sign-up confirmation is on, and it answers two problems rather than one** (owner's call, stated by him after the first real sign-up).

  **A typo costs nothing when it is caught here and everything when it is not.** Recovery reaches any address on file, confirmed or not — so a *correct* address needs no confirmation. The case that breaks is a mistyped one: the reset mail goes somewhere real and unreachable, and the diver finds out on the day they have already lost the password. With confirmation on, the same typo surfaces at registration, before anything depends on it, and **costs nothing at all — the dives never left the phone**, because §1 makes the account optional and the logbook local. There is no state in which a confirmed-address failure loses data.

  **And an unconfirmed account squats an address that is not yours.** Without confirmation anyone can register any address: the real owner can then never use it, and every future recovery mail and notification for that account goes to a stranger. Confirmation reduces the exposure to **one email that does nothing unless its recipient acts** — which is the floor, not a compromise.

  **The consequence, since it removes a design sketched twice:** Supabase's confirmation **gates the first sign-in**, so there is no interval in which an unconfirmed diver is using the account and could be shown a banner about it. Nor could such a banner offer anything — with confirmation off no confirmation mail is ever sent, `resend` needs a pending one, and `updateUser` refuses the address it already holds, so the warning would be permanent and unactionable. One email, at registration, once.

  **The free tier's real email limit is volume, not capability** — the built-in mailer allows a few messages an hour, project-wide. Irrelevant at one diver, and a reason to expect §8's Pro line sooner than the database will force it. **Apple and Google come after the v1 release** (#13, moved out of v1 on 2026-11-19) and neither needs Pro: Google is a free Cloud project, Apple comes with the developer account required to ship at all.
- **Every client names its own redirect; the Site URL is only a fallback** (M2). Supabase has two settings that look alike and are not: **Redirect URLs** is an allowlist of where a link *may* land, and **Site URL** is where it lands when the caller says nothing. A fresh project's Site URL is `http://localhost:3000`, which is how Ponor's first real confirmation mail sent its diver to a dead page holding a valid access token — everything worked, nothing looked like it had. So `signUp` passes `ponor://` explicitly and so must every auth call that follows. **This is what keeps the web app (§9) from being a conflict**: two clients cannot share one default, but they can each name their own, and the allowlist holds both. Site URL is set to the app scheme only because the app is currently the only client; it becomes the web address when there is a web address, and by then nothing depends on it.
- **PostGIS** stores site coordinates and answers "sites near me"; **pg_trgm** powers fuzzy autocomplete and duplicate detection; **unaccent** folds the accents off both sides of every name comparison, so `zelezna` finds `Železná` on the server as it does on the device (M2j) — the map, the dedupe and the folding are features of the database, not app code.
- **Community model:** any signed-in user can add a site or center; rows are never hard-deleted. Bad entries are fixed by an admin setting `status` to `merged` with `merged_into` pointing at the survivor. Dives store a name snapshot, so history never breaks.
  **A merge moves the pointer and never the snapshot** (M2r). §6 gives a dive a `site_name` for history and a `site_id` for identity, and a merge is a statement about identity alone — so every dive pointing at the merged row is **repointed to the survivor** and goes on reading the spelling it was recorded with. Chosen over resolving at read time because `site_id` already has four readers and the cost of a fifth forgetting the rule is a dive quietly ungrouped. **Centres merge the same way**, on this sentence's own wording — a merged centre otherwise splits one trip into two, just as silently. **A `hidden` row is not a merge**: it has no survivor to point at, so nothing is repointed.
  **There is no un-merge.** The repoint is what makes that true: the original pairing is gone from the device once it runs, and only the name snapshot records what was there. An admin reversing `status` in Studio does not bring the old pointer back.
- **A guest creates sites and centres too, and they reach the community at sign-in** (issue #19, reversing §2.3's *"only a signed-in diver is offered it"*). The row is written on the phone, dirty, exactly as a signed-in diver's created out of signal is; nothing pushes it while there is no account. The first sign-in's adoption (§7.4) sends it up with the logbook — `adoptDiveSites` / `adoptDiveCenters` were already in it, and the server stamps `created_by` as it does for any push — and §7.1's fuzzy recheck meets it as it meets any site created offline. **That adoption flags every catalogue row on the phone, which is correct only while a guest's phone holds no community rows.** It is true today because `anon` can read neither the tables nor the search RPCs, so a guest's picker holds their own places and nothing else. **#28 (guests reading the catalogue) must narrow the adoption to the guest's own rows first** — as it stands, the first sign-in after a guest had downloaded the catalogue would flag every community row for the push, as though that diver had made it.
- **One canonical record per site:** the creator and the admin edit its facts (pin, entry, salinity, site depth); everyone else taps *"suggest a correction"*, which lands in a review queue — **`site_edits`, read by its own author and by the admin in Studio, and not by the site's creator** (M2c). A suggestion is free text from an identified diver, and routing that to another diver is a moderation surface §9 defers as a class; the creator's editing rights are over the site's facts, not over other people's messages. Opening it to the creator later is one policy.
- **Deleting an account leaves its sites standing, and nobody can edit them again** (M2c, and this is the sentence §5 was missing). §8 requires in-app deletion; the rule above requires community rows to survive, since other divers' dives point at them. So a departed diver's dives, presets, certifications and profile cascade away and their sites, centres and suggestions stay with `created_by` set null. **A null matches no `auth.uid()`, so those sites become editable by nobody through the app — including the same person signing up again.** That is coherent: they have become community records, corrected through `suggest_site_edit` like anyone else's. It is also permanent, and worth stating before someone reads it as a bug. Divers who disagree with a pin still get precision — a dive can carry its own optional GPS point, and the personal map prefers it.
- **Offline dedupe:** the compact site/center catalogue syncs to every device (all of it while the community is young, country-scoped once it isn't), so autocomplete works fully offline. **The OpenStreetMap import (#76) may end the first on day one**: measuring its size is that issue's first step, and decides it. When a site created offline is pushed, the server reruns the fuzzy check and flags likely duplicates for a one-tap merge by the creator — admin merge is the backstop.
- **Seeding from OpenStreetMap, before release** (#76; the owner's call, 2026-11-19, in #22). The catalogue was planned community-grown first, with open data topping it up one day; it moved because a picker that is empty for the first divers in a place is what a stranger meets on day one. Issue #31 looked at the sources (2026-11-17): **Wikidata** is CC0 and safe to mix, but thin; **OpenStreetMap** has good dive-centre coverage under the ODbL, whose share-alike commits the catalogue to an open licence; **wreck datasets** ask for attribution only.
  **The licence is decided** (the owner's call, #31): places divers add are published under the ODbL (§8) — OpenStreetMap's own licence, so an import from it stays possible without asking every contributor, and anyone reusing the catalogue must share theirs back.
  **How a re-import would work is recorded, not built.** Each imported place keeps — **server-side, in a table of its own** — which source and id it came from and **the values that source gave at the last import**. A re-import then decides per field: ours unchanged since the last import → take the source's new value; ours changed (a suggestion or an admin edit) and the source's unchanged → keep ours; both changed → keep ours, and file the source's value as a suggestion in #54's queue. No edit has to record anything for this to work, and keeping it off the place rows keeps it out of every phone's sync, since `pull_changes` sends whole rows. That is also why #31 added no provenance column: it arrives with the import, on the server.
- **RPC functions** (Postgres, callable from the client): `search_sites`, `search_centers`, `similar_sites`, `suggest_site_edit`, `pull_changes`, `push_changes`, `delete_account`. No Edge Functions needed for v1.
