It is on every chosen mark rather than only the community one, so a focused site and a pressed place are visibly one state; the outlined two invert as well, as they did. The rest of the canvas's map — its pin shapes, its filter chips — is still not this change.
- **A site and a centre are chosen on a page, and every name on a dive is a site** (2026-11-15, issue #19; the owner's calls, made in conversation over the design). Logging dives on TestFlight, the owner typed a site name and got no suggestions, added it and saw nothing happen, typed it again and still found nothing, and on a second *Add* was asked whether he meant it: *"I would expect I will get the site in search results immediately."* The issue traced five defects, and the first was a spec asserting an unbuilt feature once more — §2.3 promised the form the on-device catalogue and live search, and the form had only ever searched the diver's own past dives. He asked for a redesign rather than a patch; §2.3 and §0.6 have it as built. His calls, each of which could have gone the other way:
  - **A page, not a list under the field or a sheet, with the search at the top.** The page is a route pushed over the form, as Search and a site's page are: the form stays mounted so nothing typed is lost, swipe-back works at every step, and the create pages can be reached from elsewhere later. The cost is a small hand-off, since a route returns nothing (`navigation/placePick.ts`). A full-screen overlay owned by the form would have returned the pick for free, and lost the native back gesture at every step inside it, met the keyboard and map quirks iOS overlays have, and added to a 4,500-line screen; the canvas's inline list under the field went when the page was chosen. The search at the top is deliberately not the Dives search's bottom dock (§0.6).
  - **One list, grouped by *have you dived here?*, with community places at any distance.** Not by distance: back-filling logs at home is the case that shaped the picker, and there the phone is nowhere near the dive.
  - **Every name is a site** — no free text and no *"just this dive"* row. It reverses §2.3's *"§6's snapshot is the ordinary case, not a degraded one"* for new dives, and it closes the fifth defect by construction: the row has no keyboard, so there is no typed name to leave unlinked. A quieter private path can be added later if a real need appears.
  - **Guests create** local places, published when they sign in — reversing *"only a signed-in diver is offered it"* (§2.3, §5). One line on a guest's create page and one on the *Create* row, and no other prompt: an account is optional (§1), and a prompt under the most-used field would be a nag on every dive.
  - **Guests should see the community catalogue too** — #28, after this, because the adoption has to be narrowed first (§5).
  - ***Near me* as a pill, and the map picker wanted — split to #30** (§9), since #21 was rewriting the map code at the time.
  - **Creating opens a page with the place's facts**, not a name alone.
  - **The selected value is always visible under the title, worded *Selected:*** — *"on this dive"* was rejected as unclear — **and the title is *Choose a site*.**
  - **Dives that carry a name and no id are not designed for** — test data, which the owner deletes. So the picker offers no history-only names: a name with no catalogue row is neither under *Dived here* nor a ready-made *Create* row. PR #35's code review raised it (a guest's pre-#19 dives would each need their site created again) and the owner's call stands: no such logbook exists outside the owner's test account.
  - **His calls on the nine questions PR #35 left open** (2026-11-15, made in its code review):
    - **A centre's *Dived with* row carries no figure.** *"The only one which would make the sense is number of dives. But I think its not necessary."* The canvas's screen 05 draws a muted `used` in that slot; the heading already says it. Earlier text here said the canvas draws nothing there, which was wrong. #37 brings the canvas in line with this and the other deliberate departures.
    - **No accuracy on the position card** — *"No accuracy, lets drop that."* `currentPosition` reports none, and M2l already declined showing one beside a stored pin: here it would be honest only until Save, since no column keeps it.
    - **A position copied from the dive is changed, not cleared** — *"there should be a way how to change it to different location."* No clear ring. *Use current position* changes it now; the map does once #30 gives the create pages *Choose on map*; typed or pasted coordinates went to §9's shelf, reversing M1i's *"no one will ever type it manually"* for the create pages only.
    - **The *Near [site]* row's line says where the place came from**, as every other row's does. It read `3.4 km from Shark Point`, which said the distance trailing the row and the site in the heading a second time.
    - **A near-match on Save opens the site's own page, with *Use this site*** — *"the user should also be able to 'preview' the site detail, so he can check if its really a duplicate."* The match also shows its country and its distance from the page's position.
    - **No chevron on that line, and §0.6 stays whole** — *"Drop it."* The PR's own question had recommended a trailing chevron so the line would read as tappable, and the option put to the owner described a chevron as how other pressable rows say they go somewhere. That misdescribed the app's convention: §0.6 spends the chevron on in-place disclosure and never on navigation, and the same rule already took the canvas's chevrons off the dive detail's map card and *Imported from* (above). It was built for one round and taken out. How a row says it can be pressed is #5's to decide, for every such row at once rather than one at a time.
    - **The picker's search and the create page's *Name* capitalise the first letter only** — *"Its not meant to be only for Czech people."* Most languages do not title-case a place name, and a created name is published to the community catalogue; *Kamena vrata*, not *Kamena Vrata*. A buddy and a guide on the dive form keep word capitals: they are people's names.
    - **A guest's near-miss duplicates wait for #28.** A guest's check catches only identical names, because the near-misses come from `similar_sites`, which a guest cannot ask; #28 is asked to open it to guests with the catalogue. Nothing changed here.
    - The new iOS location prompt rides with the next TestFlight build, and the Czech wording stands as built.
- **What building the picker decided, each checkable against the code** (2026-11-15, issue #19; calls made in the design and in review rather than by the owner, and his to overturn):
  - **A new place is never given the phone's position unasked.** A site's position starts as the dive's own pin when the dive has one, a centre's always empty, and *Use current position* is the only way the phone's gets in. The prototype pre-fills the phone's position — which at home publishes a dive site at the diver's house.
  - **Near me takes `roughPosition`, a second owner beside `currentPosition` for a different question** (§4.1). A 50 km list that stores nothing wants a fix quickly and takes a coarse one: a last-known position up to ten minutes old, else a balanced fix within ten seconds, and Precise Location off still works. Refusing a 500 m fix as `imprecise` would leave a diver on a ferry with a dead button. A flag on `currentPosition` was the alternative, and the two disagree on every parameter and on what a refusal is; the permission check is the one function both call. Near me words its refusals for a list, and the two controls that take a stored pin share `format/positionRefusal.ts`, so the GPS row's sentences have one owner.
  - **50 km for Near me, 10 km for *Near [site]*, and *Near [site]* lists any provenance.** A centre the diver added and never dived with is as near as any other; those already under *Dived with* are left out, so no row appears twice.
  - ***Near you* carries no count**, where the other groups are counted while something is typed: its heading already states a bound, and the design draws none.
  - **"Nothing within 50 km" only once the catalogue has answered.** An unread table and an empty one are the same `[]`, and a failed read counts as resolved (`db/liveQuery.ts`), so a page that could not read the catalogue must not also call it empty. **Nor while the server is still being asked about the fix** (PR #35's code review): a signed-in diver whose phone held none of the nearby community sites read *Nothing within 50 km* the moment the fix landed, contradicted a moment later by the rows the server sent. The Near me supplement now reports whether its question is out, and the page says *Looking for places within 50 km…* until it is not. Out of signal the server "answers" at once (`searchCatalogue` turns every failure into `[]`), so a diver at sea still gets the sentence.
  - **Every way off a page happens once.** A second tap on a picker row would complete the pick again, and its second `dismiss` would pop the dive form itself; a second on *Create* would stack two create pages. **Since PR #35's code review the registry absorbs the first of those itself**: a token completed or forgotten is *settled*, and completing a settled token does nothing, while a token it never saw (a reload) still closes the screens — so the next completer (the map picker, #30) cannot reach the form twice whatever its screen keeps. The screens' claims stay, because the other ways off are plain navigation and not idempotent: a `‹ Cancel` landing during a pick's dismiss would go back past the form, and a pick landing during the *Create* push would dismiss the create page and strand the picker on a settled token. So a pick, *No centre*, `‹ Cancel` and *Create* claim one ref and do nothing once it is claimed, and the claim is released when the picker is focused again — *Create*'s `‹ Back` comes back to it. The create page does the same for `‹ Back`, a match and a save that went through. On the form one claim covers both place rows and is released when the form is focused again — **not a lock in the pick registry**, because a request the registry never heard forgotten would wedge it until a reload, while focus always comes back.
  - **The whole row is the press, and the ring stands on any held place.** A label dead to a tap was fixed on the form once already (§0.6, *the whole row is the field*), and review found it again here. The ring empties name and id together and leaves `— cleared` only for a carried value, as on a typed row, and *No centre* empties by the same rule; without the ring a mistaken pick could only be replaced.
  - **The caption under a paired name is the stored ISO code, or *on this phone only* under a guest's row** — not under a signed-in diver's unpushed row, which is on its way and reads its country. Whether a session exists decides it, so a keychain that has not answered yet reads as a guest for a frame: that costs a caption, never a gesture.
  - **What is saved is what is on screen.** Save is shut while the device is still being asked for a position, the fields are held still from Save until a question or a failure hands them back, and a new position forgets a standing question as a new name does, since the server's check was asked around the old one. A failed write has its own sentence — *"Nothing on the dive has changed"* — because the form's *"the dive keeps the name"* was false on a page that never gave the dive one.
  - **§2.3's exact-match sentence is drawn for the first time.** §2.3 says an exact match and a near-match get different sentences; the form asked *Did you mean "Kotelna"?* about an identical name, and its own docblock reported the gap for §2.3 rather than rewording it. The create page draws *"Kotelna" is already a site.* Moving the check is what caught it, not a test.
  - **Under a kilometre a distance is metres, and never under ten** (the whole-branch review). A diver standing at the site read `0.0 km`, which says the place is nowhere. Under a kilometre — half a mile in imperial — the figure is metres (feet) to the nearest ten, floored at ten, because a fix is not good to a metre and `0 m` would be the same false claim in a smaller unit; decided on the rounded figure, so 996 m reads `1.0 km`.
  - **A picker with nothing to list says that typing is the way in** (review). A new diver opened a title, a search and a pill; with no place listed, nothing typed and *Near me* off, one muted line now stands where rows would be, as the Search screen's empty field has one — and not before both reads have answered, so it never flashes over *Dived here* rows about to arrive.
  - **Same-day ties in *Dived here* follow the logbook's order, not the alphabet** (review). Rows sort by their latest dive under `compareDiveOrder` — date, time in, hand order, the order logged — so two sites dived on one day stand as the dive numbers put them; the catalogue's own order decides only between rows whose latest dive is the same dive.
  - **A duplicate is offered only once the phone holds its row** (PR #35's code review). `similar_sites` answers whole sync rows; `findSiteTwins` writes them with `applyPulledDiveSites` before offering them, and drops a server match the phone still does not hold — a row the strict sync reader refuses, or a write that failed. The matches themselves are still read loosely, so a newer server never breaks the check; only the storing is strict.
  - **The dive being edited is not its own history** (PR #35's code review). The form already left the dive under edit out of its suggestions; the picker counted it, so a diver putting right a dive paired with the wrong site — their only dive there — found that site under *Dived here*, counted from the very pairing being undone. The form now tells the picker which dive it is editing (`DiveContext.diveId`, null for a new dive), and the picker leaves it out of *Dived here* / *Dived with* and their counts; the *Selected* line still says what the dive holds now. Planned dives stay out as before (§2.3: a plan is not a visit).
  - **Save never waits on the network for longer than it would for a country** (review). `findSiteTwins` gives `similar_sites` four seconds (`TWIN_CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS`) and goes on with the device's answer; the Supabase client sets no fetch timeout, and every site on a dive now passes through that check. And the country is asked once: the create page hands the answer its *Country* row shows, with the point it is for, and `addSite` / `addCenter` ask only when that point is not the row's own.
- **Where the picker departs from its prototype, the Claude design "Ponor site picker"** (2026-11-15, issue #19). The prototype is a sketch of the look and the flow; where it and the approved design disagree, or it draws something the design is silent on, the design governs:
  - **The deepest dive is ink, not a band colour** — an aggregate (M1l), as the canvas's suggestion rows were overruled on 2026-11-14.
  - **No *Note* on a site and no *Address* on a centre.** Neither has a column, so either is a schema change on the device and the server and a migration the owner applies; address is #31. A centre gets its existing *Website* instead, and a site gains *Water body*, which the prototype leaves out and §2.3 inherits.
  - **Countries are the stored ISO code** (`HR`), not names (*Croatia*), as every other site and centre row in the app shows them (§2.3, M2o).
  - **The chips are the app's values**: salt and fresh — *brackish* went in M1h — and shore, boat and other.
  - **A centre's position starts empty**, where the prototype pre-selects one from a map pin.
  - **A guest sees their own places only** (#28), where an early version shows them community rows; a guest's new row reads *on this phone only* where the prototype says *added by you*.
  - **The *Map* pill and *Choose on map* are #30**; the sheet variant and the bottom-docked search were alternatives, and the page with search on top was chosen over them.
- **The tab bar stays native and takes the canvas's glyphs** (2026-11-16, issue #2, the owner's call). The canvas draws the bar as a floating capsule whose selected tab is an inverted-ink pill, and the installed `NativeTabs` cannot draw that pill on iOS: its selection colour (`indicatorColor`) is Android's and the browser's only, and iOS 26 draws its own glass bubble. Three options were prototyped as the Claude design "Ponor tab bar", from real simulator screens in both themes: **(A)** stay native and adopt the glyphs; **(B)** a bar of our own drawn to the canvas, which reverses §3's call and gives up the shrink-on-scroll, the glass lens and the system's tab accessibility, and needs a 104 pt bottom clearance on every tab screen; **(C)** change nothing. The owner chose A. Both A and B were checked to need no new native build (template images go through Metro; the Sep-10 dev build already holds the SVG and glass modules), so the choice was the look and what native buys, not cost — though the owner's later call on vectors (below) made A need one after all. §0.6 has the bar as built and its departures from the canvas.
  - **The Dives glyph is the mark redrawn small, not the mark scaled down.** The canvas's 22-unit drawing has one stop on the ascent where §0.3's mark has two; at tab size the second stop and the long wave are detail that turns to a smudge. It has its own source (`assets/tab-mark.svg`), one ink only — `plugins/withTabGlyphs.js` refuses a second — and its browser PNGs are held by `markAsset.test.ts` to the first-run mark's claim — one colour over a transparent ground, one drawing at 1×, 2× and 3× — and to the SVG itself: the test re-renders each through the build's own renderer and compares, so a redrawn SVG without `npm run icons:build` fails CI instead of leaving the browser on the old glyph.
  - **Ponor's glyphs are vectors in the native apps, not PNGs** (the owner's call on PR #38). Shipped as template PNGs they needed no native build and drew identically at tab size — UIKit never scales a tab image — but a vector is what iOS's accessibility magnifier enlarges cleanly, and the owner chose it knowing the cost. A tab bar cannot be handed an SVG at runtime, only an image NAME, so a config plugin (`plugins/withTabGlyphs.js`) writes each SVG into the iOS asset catalog (`preserves-vector-representation`, `template-rendering-intent: template` — the intent is stated, though the tint itself comes from the bar's icon colours, `iconColor` at rest and `tintColor` selected, as review found) and into an Android `VectorDrawable`, both from the same SVG through the same parser; the parser reads only the shapes and attributes these glyphs use and fails the prebuild on anything else — another element, a `transform` or `opacity`, a shape both stroked and filled, one silent about its fill — so neither platform can be handed a drawing that differs from the SVG. **The cost:** every binary from before the plugin draws Dives and Settings with no glyph, so the change waits on a new EAS build. Checked on the simulator with a local prebuild-and-Xcode build; Android's drawables are unit-tested only.
  - **Settings is the canvas's two sliders** (the owner's call on PR #38), over iOS's `slider.horizontal.3`, which drew three. Ponor's drawing, by the same route as the mark.
  - **28 pt is a measurement.** UIKit draws a tab image at its own size, so the size is chosen rather than inherited; at the canvas's 22 the mark drew visibly smaller than the SF Symbols beside it (measured about 23–24 pt wide in iOS 26's bar), and at 28 its ink matches them.
  - **Android's selection pill is inverted ink, unchecked** (the owner's call on PR #38). Material draws a pill behind the selected icon and `indicatorColor` sets it, so on Android the canvas's pill is within reach: it is `action`, and the selected icon `action-fg` — on Android only, since on iOS that ink would vanish into the pale glass. Nothing here can show an Android screen, so it ships having been seen by no one; it wants a look on one.
  - **The mark's wave keeps full ink** (the owner's call on PR #38), as the canvas draws it, rather than the first-run mark's half-alpha wave; at rest on the glass a half-ink wave goes faint.
- **The code is source-available under PolyForm Noncommercial 1.0.0** (2026-11-16, issue #41). The owner wanted others to be free to read, change and share the code, but not to use it commercially without his permission. No open-source licence as OSI defines one can forbid commercial use, so a source-available licence was chosen. **The alternatives it beat:** MIT, which allows commercial use; GPL-3.0, which allows it as long as the result stays open; CC BY-NC 4.0, which forbids it but whose authors advise against using it for software; and no licence at all, which forbids even non-commercial reuse. The licence binds others, not the owner: he still publishes Ponor in the stores and can grant commercial licences. **The cost:** code someone else contributes stays theirs, so relicensing it later, or licensing it commercially, needs their agreement, unless a contributor agreement is asked for first. **The required notice** (the line every copy must carry) names the owner and links this repo, with no year: a year adds no protection, and the link is how someone wanting commercial use finds him. It can point at `ponor.app` once a site there offers a way to reach him; copies already shared keep the old line, and the repo link keeps working.
- **The empty logbook offers an account, and only there and at the top of Settings** (2026-11-16, issue #24, the owner's calls on the design canvas). Three placements were weighed: a control on the first-run screen; a one-time card on the list after the first dive is saved, when the benefit is concrete; or both. The owner chose the first-run screen alone. The card after a save would add a dismissible card to the most-used screen and need a "comes back or not" rule, and §0.6 had already said a guest gets "no banner, no prompt". The first-run screen is also what a diver with an account sees on a new phone or after signing out, so the offer is their way back too. **The order is the owner's: the primary, then `or`, then the card.** A first draft had the card above the button, and it crowded the teaching block. **The wording is the owner's.** *Back up with an account* was turned down because on that screen there is nothing to back up yet. The card says first that Ponor is better with an account and that it is optional, and that the app works fully without one. The offer has nothing to dismiss and no count of how often it was shown; signing in is what removes it. **§0.5 gives way on this one screen:** with the card the primary sits mid-screen. The owner accepted that, since a diver sees the screen once and mostly on land. **Settings carries the same card at its top for a guest**, and drops its *Account* row while the card shows. `offersAccount` is the one owner of "is this a guest" — a pure function each screen asks of its own one session read, never a second subscription (review of PR #52): a backend exists, the session read has answered, and nobody is signed in. A `null` session before the read would otherwise offer a sign-in to a diver who is signed in. **The mail limit becomes real here:** every new diver meets the offer, so #14 (the branded, unthrottled auth mail) now blocks the store release (§9).
- **The account screen takes the canvas's grouped layout, and with it the one larger title in the app** (2026-11-16, issue #24, the owner's pick of two drawn options). The canvas drew two options. **A** was grouped: a welcome head, both rows in one surface card, and every control a button shape. **B** drew each field as its own bordered box, with the mode on a switch at the top. A kept the row grammar the dive form uses; B would have added a second field grammar to the app. **Three earlier rulings give way, each knowingly** — the owner confirmed them on PR #52, along with the first-run order that bends §0.5. (1) M2e kept this screen's title at every title's 20, arguing that a sign-in screen is where a title quietly grows a size of its own. The owner found the screen boring and chose a 28 pt bold head, so `styles.test.ts` now pins that one exception rather than the old rule. (2) The muted-label treatment for the secondary acts gave way to **the outlined button**, the app's first — on this screen *Sign out* is outlined in full ink rather than M1d's "the app's own control stays muted", though it is still no louder than a drawn outline and the weight still goes into the platform's dialog. It is a hairline in `border` round the primary's box, with the label in full ink: no hue (§0.1), and drawn rather than filled, so the inverted primary is still the screen's one purpose. It is one definition for the mode switch, *Back to sign in*, *Sign out* and the offer card's button. (3) *Forgot password?* became §0.6's quiet pill, because as a muted label under the password row it read as a third row. **What did not change:** a refusal still stands under the row it is about, now inside the card (the canvas draws it under the card; the M2 rule that found an email error pointing two rows up wins). Sign-out stays at the end of the content. The way out reads *‹ Back*, by M3k's rule, now that the first-run card is a second way in.
- **The first-run screen trims its teaching block rather than hide its one primary action** (2026-11-16, review round 2 of PR #52, the owner's call). Once the account offer moved *Log your first dive* into the scroll (so that, at large text sizes, the card could not end up out of reach), the button itself could start below the fold on a small phone. M1h made the teaching block the screen's content, and that still holds where there is room. Where there is not, the reason lines go first and the mark second, because the six bars alone still teach that colour is depth. It is measured rather than predicted from the text size: a guess per device and language would be wrong somewhere, and the screen can see where it actually drew the button. It never steps back up from a trimmed layout, because the layout that fits only after trimming would stop fitting the moment it was untrimmed.
- **Sites and centres get every planned field at once** (2026-11-17, issue #31; the owner's calls, made on the issue and on the canvas board *Open · issue #31 · round 3*, which he approved before any code). The issue began as one optional *Address* on a dive centre, which #19 shipped without for want of a column. He widened it to every field planned for a site or a centre, so that the server change is applied once and the build installed once rather than once per field; what happens to a place after it is made stays in issues of its own (#54–#57). The fields are what dive directories show (researched 2026-11-17), chosen to still make sense beside #43's reviews, and none is a figure a diver's own logs can compute — those need no column and stay with #43. §6 has the columns, §0.6 the pages, §2.3 the search and the duplicate check, §5 and §8 the licence. His calls:
  - **A set is a JSON array and a single choice is a vocabulary column.** `site_types`, `services` and `agencies` may each hold several values — a wreck on a wall — so they are stored exactly as `dives.equipment` is, `'[]'` and never null; `suitable_for` holds one. All are plain text inside, with no enum and no CHECK, by the store-and-flag ruling: a token from a newer client is kept, read as a word rather than a key, and never dropped by a press on the chips beside it (`TokenChips`).
  - **Nine site types, the owner's call (2026-11-17): `wreck · reef · wall · pinnacle · canyon · swim_through · muck · drift · blue_water`** — the place's shapes first, then what the water does. *Canyon* is a cleft cut down into the bed or reef that you swim along. *Swim-through* (*Průplav*), added at his call after the first eight, is an arch or short passage, open at both ends, that you swim through — a shape of the place, so it stands with the shapes. *Blue water* is open water with no bottom in reach, dived for pelagic life; it is named *Blue water*, not *Open ocean*, so it never reads as the water body *Ocean*. *Cave* is not a type and stays a water body (sea caves can be salt, #53). No migration: a type is plain text inside a JSON array, with no enum and no CHECK.
  - **A site's entry is a set, under its old name** (the owner's call, 2026-11-17, made in #6's session and carried into this issue so that the server change is still applied once): *"The site offers what is possible; the dive logs which one was done."* A site entered from the shore and from a boat is both, so `dive_sites.entry` holds every way in — `shore · boat · other`, the dive's own vocabulary — stored as the other sets are, `'[]'` and never null; a dive's `entry` stays the one way it used. **Changed in place rather than renamed**, while the project holds test data only (M3p's precedent): the singular name keeps `suggest_site_edit`'s list, the sync payload and every reader on the one key, at the cost of a plural fact under a singular word. The device makes each stored value a set before drizzle-kit's rebuild copies it — a custom migration that runs first, since a copied null would fail the NOT NULL and a copied `shore` is no set — and `supabase/README.md` has the server's `alter column`. **§2.1's prefill takes a site's entry only when it offers exactly one way in**: with several the site cannot say which this dive used, so it says nothing and the entry is left to the diver and carry-over. A new site inherits the dive's one entry as a set of one (§2.3), and the create page asks it as a set. Picking the dive's entry from its site's options, with the site's facts folded away on the dive form, is #63.
  - **A site's altitude is its own column, offered from the GPS fix** (the owner's call, 2026-11-17, approved in #6's session and carried here so that the server change is still applied once): `dive_sites.altitude_m`, nullable metres, negative below sea level, saved to the whole metre. It is a *quantity* of its own in `format/units.ts` rather than a depth: no band colour (§0.1), no refusal of a negative, no decimal. `currentPosition` already holds the fix, so it returns the altitude beside the pair instead of a second location call. **The fix's altitude is offered, never written** (the owner's pick on the board *#31 · Site defaults, altitude offer*, 2026-11-17: *"it will be common a user logs a dive somewhere else than the dive site, so the GPS will not tell us anything useful"*): a press that yields one puts *Use GPS altitude · 12 m* under the row, and only a tap fills it (§0.6). That withdraws the first build's ruling that each such press overwrites the Altitude row. **The fix's altitude is kept only on iOS, and only when the receiver vouches for it:** expo-location on Android passes height above the WGS 84 ellipsoid (about +45 m on the Adriatic) where iOS's `CLLocation.altitude` is above mean sea level, so on Android the row is typed-only until #64 converts it; and only with a vertical accuracy above 0 and at most 50 m (`COARSEST_USABLE_ALTITUDE_ACCURACY_M` — altitude diving's first step is 300 m, so ±50 m still says which side of it a site is). A device that reports no accuracy never offers one; the diver types it. A typed altitude reads as a whole number with thousands grouping (`3,812` is 3812, not 3.8; `parseAltitudeMetres`, `format/units.ts`). Showing an altitude on a *dive*, from about 300 m up where altitude diving starts, is #63, not this issue.
  - ***Suitable for* is typed, not computed**, and named by what a diver can handle rather than by one agency's course: a shallow site with a strong current is still *advanced*. Filtering by it against the diver's wallet is not this issue (§9).
  - **A centre's agencies are a closed list with *Other*, where a diver's own certification agency is free text** (M3b). The difference is whose fact it is. A closed list in the wallet would tell a diver their own card is unrecognised; a centre's agencies are something other divers filter on, and *Other* still lets a centre of an unlisted agency say it trains. M3b's own warning applies: a precedent is an argument, not a template, and read a second time the wallet's points the other way here.
  - **No pin from an address; the address gives the country** — the one exception to §2.3's *"from the row's own pin and from nothing else"*, and the reason `platform/geocode.ts` and `addCenter` cite. A looked-up address often finds only the town, and a stored pin claims precision (M2l's 100 m rule): town-centre pins would put centres in the wrong street for everyone and skew *Near [site]*. A country is the one thing a vague address still gets right, so it is evidence of a country the way a pin is — and the rule it bends was written against guesses, the diver's locale or the last site they logged, which an address is not. It waits for a granted permission on Android rather than asking for one because the platform's geocoder needs it there, and nothing typed into an address box may raise a system prompt. A pin still comes from *Use current position*, and later from #30's map.
  - **A centre's address is a row under *Contact*, not part of the header line** the canvas's screen 12 draws; **the address, the number and the email open the maps, the dialler and the mail app with no mark**, as the website row always has — how a pressable row says so is #5's.
  - **The description is public, near the top, and says so.** It replaces the *Note* the canvas drew, sits straight under the name (and a centre's address) because it is what a diver opens a place to learn, and is labelled *everyone can read it* so nobody writes a private note there.
  - **Not added:** the languages a centre speaks and its social links, which its website covers; and provenance for imported rows, which lives server-side and arrives with an import (§5).
  - **A guess through another name says which** (the owner's call, 2026-11-17, settling what the PR had left open). Typing *Kamena vrat* finds *Stone Gate* through its other name *Kamena vrata*, and *Did you mean “Stone Gate”?* alone gave the diver nothing tying it to what was typed; the page now asks *Did you mean “Stone Gate” (also called Kamena vrata)?* — *Nemysleli jste „Stone Gate“ (jiným názvem Kamena vrata)?*, in the picker's own Czech for *also called* (`picker.alsoCalled`; the controller's wording fix, 2026-11-17). Only the server can say which name came near: the device words a match by folded equality against the row it holds (`twinRelation`), which a near match never meets. So `similar_sites` adds a `matched_name` key to each row it returns, beside `sync_site`'s rendering — a key in the returned JSON, not a column, so its signature is unchanged and nothing more is dropped. It holds the site's other name that scored best (`best_alt_name`, as stored and trimmed, a tie going to the one listed first) when the site's other names against the name typed is the best of the three scores the rank is taken from, and beats both of the others outright; a tie, or a match through the site's own name either way round, holds null and reads *Did you mean “Stone Gate”?* as before. The three scores are written once, in `scores`, and the filter, the rank and the answer all read them there (the review's fix, 2026-11-17), so none of them can drift from the others. The device reads the key loosely — a non-blank string, or nothing — carries it as `SiteTwin.alsoCalled` and never stores it; the device's own exact finding on another name keeps its sentence, *“Kamena vrata” is another name for Stone Gate.*
  - **A site's page is one *The site* cluster, with a note naming the rows that fill in a new dive** (the owner's pick C on the board *#31 · Site defaults, altitude offer*, 2026-11-17, settling the call the PR had left open). Three were drawn, for a site with one way in and one with two, in both themes: **A**, as built — a *Site defaults* cluster of its own under a caption; **B**, the canvas — one cluster and nothing said, so the page never says these values fill in a new dive; **C**, the canvas's one cluster and one muted line under it naming which of its rows do. He picked C. It withdraws the build's own placement — one way in under *Site defaults*, several among the facts (the controller's ruling in review) — so a site's ways in are always one *Entry* row now, and the prefill's rule (`siteDefaultValue`) decides only whether the note names it: exactly one way in. Salinity and water body became one *Water* row, as the canvas draws them; the *Site defaults* heading and its caption went (§0.6).
  - **Places divers add are published under the ODbL** (§8, for #15's terms), so an OpenStreetMap import stays possible without asking every contributor; how a re-import would treat our edits is recorded in §5, not built.
  - **Calls made while building it, each checkable against the code and his to overturn:**
    - **Multi-word vocabulary values are spelled in snake_case — `open_water`, `sdi_tdi`, `swim_through`, `blue_water`** (the owner's call, 2026-11-17, replacing the first build's `openwater` / `sditdi` / `swimthrough` / `bluewater`). This replaces M1h's rule that every member is one lowercase word with no separator; `domain/types.test.ts` now pins every member to lowercase words joined by a single `_` (`/^[a-z]+(_[a-z]+)*$/`), and still rejects a space, a capital, a doubled or a leading underscore. Reason: one-word mashups do not scale as more vocabularies arrive (species #45, gear #58), and snake_case matches the column names. Every existing one-word value (`semidry`, the weather values, …) is still valid, so nothing old migrates; the four renamed values had not shipped and only test data holds them. What a diver reads is unchanged: *Open Water*, *SDI/TDI*. The CSV export's space-separated lists stay safe, since `_` is not a space.
    - **An address's country gets one time budget, and Save waits for a lookup already out.** `countryForAddress` runs the forward lookup and `countryFor`'s reverse one inside a single `GEOCODE_TIMEOUT_MS`, the wait a pin's country gets, so an address never delays Save longer than a pin does; the plan had given each call its own. The cost: a slow geocoder gives no country where it might have given one late. And Save takes a lookup still out for the very address in the box rather than asking a second time.
    - **A wreck's year is rounded at the write, as a dive's integers are.** The rounding moved out of `db/dives.ts` into `db/integers.ts`, which `db/catalogue.ts` now shares, so the `manual_order` entry's *"non-integers are rounded rather than rejected"* now covers a new catalogue row as it covers a dive. `push_changes` casts `wreck_sunk_year` to `integer`, and a fraction would fail the diver's whole push (§7). The server's cast stays: only some other client could now send it a fraction, and `max_depth_m`'s cast, to `double precision`, has always failed a push the same way when the value is not a number. The create page takes digits only.
    - **`sync_json_array` is how a set reaches its NOT NULL column.** `push_changes` reads the community tables key by key out of the payload, so nothing between a client's JSON and the column supplies the `'[]'` default: another client's `null`, or a string where a list belongs, would refuse the diver's whole push. Anything that is not an array is stored as `[]`. It is not `strict`, since a strict function answers a null with null, the one input it exists to turn into `[]`.
    - **Only strings are names, on both sides.** `alsoCalled` on the device and `folded_names` on the server (and `best_alt_name`, which reads the list the same way) skip a number or a `null` inside a list of other names and read a list that is not one as no names, so a row from another client's build is found the same way on the phone and on the server, and never makes a search raise.
    - **`similar_sites` changed its signature, and was dropped and re-created.** It takes `p_alt_names`, a JSON list defaulting to `[]`, and `create or replace` refuses a new argument, so file 6 drops the old signature immediately before creating the new one, as `supabase/README.md`'s *Re-running* says a signature change must. Both callers — `similarSites.ts` and `push_changes`' arrival recheck — name their arguments, so a call naming only the old ones still reaches the new function. The check runs both ways; other name against other name is not compared, because the issue names two directions and no third (§2.3).
    - **The word-similarity cut-off is taken from the floor.** A trigram index over a list of names can only hold them joined, and plain similarity against three joined names dilutes a perfect match on one. Word similarity (`%>`) asks whether the query matches some stretch of the joined text and is never below the similarity to the one name that stretch is, so pre-filtering by it at `name_match_floor()` cannot drop a row the per-name score (`alt_name_similarity`) keeps. Its GUC defaults to 0.6, twice the floor, so `search_sites` and `similar_sites` set it with `set_config(…, true)` as they run: a call, where file 6 declines a `SET` clause for `%`'s GUC because a clause depends on the GUC being registered when the file is pasted, and transaction-local, so it never stays on a pooled connection. That pg_trgm takes the value up was shown on a throwaway Postgres 16 (`supabase/README.md`).
    - **The email link is built from an allowlist.** `mailtoUrl` takes one plain address — letters, digits and `._+-` before the `@`, dot-joined labels of letters, digits and `-` after it — and nothing else. Other divers' text reaches the mail app, and a percent-encoded `,` or `?`, a line break or a `#` adds a recipient or a header in any handler that decodes before it splits, which a list of characters to keep out would miss. The cost: an unusual but valid address (an international domain, a quoted local part) is shown and cannot be pressed.
    - ***Site depth* reads its unit while empty, where the canvas writes *optional*.** §0.6's 2026-11-14 rule keeps a figure's unit on its row while it is empty, and gives that row no placeholder: a right-aligned placeholder put the caret through its last letter.
    - **The create page's *Entry* was words only** while `TokenChips` had no icon slot. Issue #6 gave it one, so the set's tiles carry the same drawings as the dive form's single *Entry* (*other* stays a word).
    - **One caption slot on the create page's *Entry*, and *from this dive* takes it** while the set is still the dive's one way in; otherwise it reads *any that apply*, as *Site type* does. The mark is the one that can stop being true, and pressing a second way in both drops it and shows what the row is.
    - **The JSON export's site `entry` became an array under `version: 1`.** Acceptable before release: no released file exists, and nothing reads one back.
    - **A site's entry reads in its stored order**, as every set does (`formatPlaceTokens`), not re-sorted into the vocabulary's. Everything this app writes is in that order already (`TokenChips`, and a set of one), so the two differ only for a list another client wrote out of order.
- **Ponor's own icon set, and where it shows** (issue #6; the owner's picks on the design canvas, category by category, 2026-11-16/17). §0.6 holds the hand and the table; these are the calls that change a screen.
    - **Visibility, current and surge carry one icon per field, the level in words: a departure from §0.6.** §0.6 gives an icon to a value with a shape, and these icons mark the field instead. The owner rejected every per-level drawing (*"I dont like any"*) and asked for one per field. On the dive detail a bare word in a row of icon tiles would break the row. The form keeps word chips for all three, so the departure lives on the detail only.
    - ***None* draws the field's own lines dashed, without heads** (the owner asked whether *None* should differ). The plain field icon on a *None* tile said there was a current. Dashed lines keep the field recognisable with the motion taken out, the same move as the average-depth glyph's dashed floor.
    - **The detail's conditions are tiles, and *Conditions* is now the first cluster**, under the map and profile cards, as the canvas's whole *Dive detail* draws it. *Date & time*, the cluster that always renders, follows it. Rows stay for what has no shape: the temperatures, the visibility distance and salinity.
    - **The suit's thickness rides on the suit's tile** (*Wet · 5 mm*, the owner's call). A thickness recorded without a suit stays a row, so it is never lost and never said twice.
    - **The detail's rating is the drawn dots** (the owner, 2026-11-17), the same `RatingDots` the dive row draws, where it printed *4 / 5*. The row announces the figure, since dots say nothing to a screen reader.
    - **A site's types, water body and ways in are tiles, on the site page and on *New dive site*** (the owner's call, 2026-11-18, as the canvas draws them). On the site page this splits #31's *Water* row: the water body is a tile and the salinity, which has no shape to draw, is a row of its own. The note under *The site* moved with them and names each by the label it is drawn with — *Salinity, Water body and Entry fill in…* — so it still points at what is on the page.
    - **Figure glyphs lead the two depth rows** on the form and the detail, in the label's muted ink, and the figures keep their band colour (§0.1). The owner found max and average depth hard to tell apart by colour alone. Times and temperatures get no glyph, since a clock or a thermometer would only repeat the label.
- **A re-import completes a dive whose profile failed to save; no transaction makes the two writes one** (2026-11-18, issue #9; the owner kept it over a transaction, on PR #70). The issue offered three ways.
  - **A transaction does not fit this data layer.** Drizzle 0.45's `transaction()` is synchronous on both drivers: better-sqlite3 refuses a callback that returns a promise, and expo-sqlite's session commits as soon as the callback returns, before any write awaited inside it has run. `createDive` and `putDiveSamples` are async, as every repository function here is. The shared `Db` type still accepts an async callback, so the broken version type-checks. Doing it right would take a second, synchronous way to insert a dive, used by import alone.
  - **Deleting the dive when its profile fails** is a third write that can fail the same way, and does nothing for an app killed between the two writes.
  - **Completing it on re-import needs neither.** The file is immutable, so the profile it holds is the one that should have been written, and the dedupe that used to work against the diver now finds the dive to finish. It covers the killed app too, and it makes the `unwritable` sentence's *Try again* true.
  - **The rules:** only a live dive, since a deleted one stays deleted (the re-import rule above); only when the dive has no series row at all, since a tombstoned row is a deletion §7 is carrying and `putDiveSamples` would undo it (the lookup that finds the dive joins its series row in any state, so this costs no second query); only the profile is written, never the dive; and the dive counts as imported on the run that completes it, because the run that failed did not count it.
  - **What it leaves:** until the diver tries again, the dive has no curve, as a hand-logged dive has none, and nothing on screen tells this file's failure apart from another `unwritable` one.
  - **A dive deleted on another device is fixed on the pull side, inside #9** (review of PR #70; the owner's call, "Do it in #9", relayed by the review session). The completion knows only this device. Say the dive was deleted on another device that never had its profile, and this device re-imports the file before that deletion reaches it. Then it writes a profile, and sync carries it up beside the dive's tombstone. Before this fix nothing marked a profile deleted when its dive's tombstone was pulled (`applyPulledRows` applies rows as they come), so the server kept a live profile for a deleted dive: never drawn, but on every device and in §8's export. **Now every pull ends by tombstoning any live profile whose dive this device holds as deleted** (`tombstoneSamplesOfDeletedDives`, §7.2), stamped as this device's own write, so the next push deletes the server's copy too. The guard first offered, completing only a dive this device has not synced yet, was turned down because it would break the main case: a signed-in diver's dive is usually pushed before they try again. **Older builds' leftovers are cleaned up on purpose:** the same sweep catches any live profile an earlier build left beside a deleted dive, on the first pull after the update, because a deleted dive's profile has no use (nothing draws it and nothing can undelete the dive) and §8's export should not carry it.
- **A failure the app cannot diagnose names no cause it cannot see** (2026-11-19, issue #8; the owner picked the wording). Three sentences ended *try again when you're online*: a sign-in, sign-up, password-reset, mail-link or new-password request that got no answer, or a 5xx (`serverUnreachable`), a pull-to-refresh sync that failed (`syncFailedMessage`), and an account deletion that did not run (`deleteAccountFailed`). None of them can tell a phone that is offline from a server that is down, and when the free Supabase project was paused the first one sent the owner off checking their own connection. **The sign-in sentence names both causes and claims neither:** *Couldn't reach the server — no connection here, or the server is down. Your logbook is safe on this phone; try again later.* / *Server se nepodařilo kontaktovat — buď tu není připojení, nebo server nefunguje. Váš deník je v bezpečí v telefonu; zkuste to později.* It answers only auth-js's `AuthRetryableFetchError`, which means no answer came back at all or the server answered with a 5xx (500–504, 520–530). Those are exactly its two causes, so naming both helps the diver check (PR #73's review corrected the first reasoning, which said only a request that never completed). **The sync and deletion sentences name none** and now end *try again later* / *zkuste to znovu později*. They fire on any failure, a server's refusal included, and their own notes already said they could not know a cause. The reassurance stays in all three. A test in `i18n/index.test.ts` bans the word *online* from every string in both languages, and Czech *až budete … připojen*, so a rephrasing such as *once you're back online* fails it too. A later string that needs *online* for something else (an online search, say) will fail it as well, on purpose: that test is the place to make room for it.
  - **And no refusal tells the diver to connect** (2026-11-19, issue #74, found in PR #73's review; the owner picked the wording). Sign-out's refusal (`unpushedChanges`) and start over's partial answer (`startOverUnpushed`) fire whenever the last push did not reach the account, whatever stopped it: the phone offline, the server down, or the server refusing a row. They said *Connect and try again* and *connect, and it goes up on the next sync*. With three possible causes they name none, as the sync and deletion sentences do, and keep what is true: *This phone has dives your account hasn't received yet. Nothing was cleared, and you're still signed in — try again later.* / *Tento telefon má ponory, které váš účet ještě nedostal. Nic se nevymazalo a jste stále přihlášeni — zkuste to znovu později.*; *Deleted on this device. Your account hasn't received the deletion yet — it goes up once a sync gets through.* / *V tomto zařízení smazáno. Váš účet zatím smazání nedostal — odejde, jakmile se synchronizace podaří.* That one first said *on the next sync* / *při příští synchronizaci*. PR #75's review noted a row the server keeps refusing never goes up on any sync (the engine retries a refused push forever), so the owner softened it to a promise true in every case. Sign-out's *try again later* is an instruction, not a promise, and stays. The same test now also bans every form of the verb: *connect*, *connects*, *connected*, *connecting*, each with *re-*, and in Czech any word on the stem *připoj-* (*připojte se*, *až se připojíte*, *připojit*, *až budete připojeni*). The nouns *connection* and *připojení* stay allowed, since the sign-in sentence names one as a cause.
- **v1 is what has to change before strangers' data exists, plus the logging flow** (2026-11-19, issue #22; the owner's calls). The plan had said v1's field set was frozen (§8) while §9 recorded it unfrozen since 2026-09-01, and #22's blocking list had gone stale. The **v1** GitHub milestone now holds the scope, and #22 says why each issue is in it.
  - **The rule is about data, not features.** After release, strangers' dives are real data and their builds keep syncing the old shape: once the server stops accepting a field, a build that still sends it stops syncing until the diver updates (#39). Today the only build in use is the owner's TestFlight one, so a reshape costs one migration and nobody's dives. So everything that reshapes or removes stored data goes in before release — the equipment field (#58), the cylinders and presets (#44), the dive's own GPS point (#39) — and new kinds of data and screen-only changes can ship in an update.
  - **The core logging flow goes in too, on the owner's wish that logging is enjoyable**: the map picker (#30), what one dive included (#63), planned dives (#40), salinity from the water body (#53), a diver's own version of a place (#55), a computer's profile on a hand-logged dive (#25) and field explanations (#46).
  - **The OpenStreetMap import moved from *one day* to before release** (#76), so a stranger's first picker is not empty. It brings guests reading the catalogue (#28), a picker that stays fast (#36) and editing with suggestions (#54), since imported places will carry mistakes. It may also end the whole-catalogue download on day one (§5): measuring it is #76's first step.
  - **Sign in with Apple and Google left v1** (#13). Email sign-in works, and Apple is mandatory only once Google is offered, so both come later, together.
  - **The App Store goes first, and Android follows.** M3's done-when changed from *both stores* to *the App Store*; Play's half of #15, the Android tab bar (#48) and Android's altitude (#64) come after.
  - **The cost is named:** this roughly doubles what stands between now and release. Most of the additions are `design` issues, each starting with a canvas board the owner approves, and most server changes are migrations the owner applies.
  - **Still after v1:** marine life (#45, new data, and its picture licences are open), public dive reports (#43, social stays last), the web admin area (#56) and the web app (#26), and screen-only design work (#4, #5, #10, #37).
- **A test that prints to `console.error` fails** (2026-11-19, issue #61). A full run was green while React printed *overlapping act() calls* nine times and *not wrapped in act(...)* once or twice, from four tests. **Three double-tap tests** fired two `fireEvent.press` inside one `act()`. Testing Library 14 made `fireEvent.press` async, with an `act()` scope of its own, so three scopes closed first-in-first-out and React complained at each. **Two tests left an update in flight:** an Account test set the shared mail-link state while its first screen was still mounted, and the Near me double-press test let the nearby search's timer answer after its last `act()`. That one fired only when the run was busy, which is why it came and went. **Two rules come out of it.** A double tap with no render between the taps goes through `src/testing/doubleTap.ts`: both taps on React Native's own press path (Pressability's `onClick`) inside one `act()`, so the second still meets the control as the first found it and only a ref can refuse it. Two awaited presses would let the `disabled` the first one renders do the refusing, and the test would pass with the ref deleted. And `src/testing/failOnConsoleError.ts` fails any test during which `console.error` was called, naming where each message was logged from. **Its hook unmounts what the test rendered before anything else runs.** Testing Library's own cleanup waits a tick *before* unmounting, outside `act()`, and a timer due in that tick updates the screen outside `act()`: a FlatList's 50 ms render batch did, on this branch, under load. With nothing between a test's end and the unmount, whatever a test left pending goes with the screen. One tick after the unmount lets anything else the test left due log before the check, so it is blamed on that test and not the next. No test provokes an error on purpose: every one that replaced `console.error` did it to assert that nothing was logged. The four whose spy checked only that now leave it to the check. The two that assert about a named message (DiveMap's duplicate key, `doubleTap`'s own) watch `console.error` without replacing it, so everything else still reaches the check. auth and supabase replace every console method and fail on any call, so they hide nothing. A test that one day means to provoke an error replaces `console.error` for itself and asserts on every call. The four tests turned out to check what they claimed: each was shown to fail with its guard removed, on `main` and on the branch. The cost was the noise, which would have hidden the next warning that did mean something. **It is the class *A harness can pass a mutation test by luck* already counts overlapping `act` scopes in**, and the first case of it that a check now refuses rather than a reviewer happening to spot.
- **A list of what a test covers, kept by hand, is a census that cannot fail** (2026-11-19, issue #49). `symbolName.test.tsx` said every `PlatformSymbol` the app ships was pinned, and listed the places with running totals. Two had shipped unpinned (the *Show on map* card's map and the record sheet's trash), and the totals disagreed with the file's own blocks. **The list is gone, and the test takes the census itself.** Every pin is a row in a table its tests iterate. A census reads the app's source with TypeScript's parser and requires two things. Every object literal with a string `ios` and a string `android` is held by a row, as the very object the app draws. And every `<SymbolView>` takes its `name` from `symbolName`, the one rule `tsc` cannot enforce: that prop also accepts a bare SF name or an object with no `web` key. A symbol added anywhere is red until its row is written, whether it comes with a call site of its own or is handed to one that exists, such as another capsule action. Three inline literals became exported constants so a row can hold them: SearchCapsule's magnifier, MapCard's map and the record sheet's trash. It is M3a's *a sweep that names the collection it iterates, and does not iterate it*, one level up: M3a tied the tab table to `TAB_ROUTES`, and this ties every table to the source.
- **Setting a parent sets its children: site, water body and salinity** (2026-11-19 and 2026-11-23, issue #53, the owner's call). Salinity feeds no calculation; the diver's question is *salt or fresh?* and the water body almost always answers it. So the water body sets it: the ocean is salt and its row is not drawn, any other water body brings fresh, captioned *from the water body*, and the diver can change that for a sea cave or a salt lake. Two questions decided how far the rule reaches. **Whose salinity counts:** a site's or a last dive's, but only when it was recorded for the same water body — a salt lake carries over as one, and its salt does not follow the diver into a river. **How long a hand-set salinity lasts:** until the water body changes; a choice made for one water does not describe the next. Then the owner took the same idea one level up: **changing the site sets what the site knows** — water body, one way in, salinity — over values the diver set by hand, and on a stored dive too when the diver moves it to another site. That reverses §2.1's first tier as M2s stated it (*the diver beats everything*), deliberately and only for what the site knows: the diver who picks a site has said where they are, and a value set for the place before belongs to that place. Everything the site doesn't know keeps the diver's value, and **the latest gesture still wins** — a row touched while the site's row is being read is the diver's when the answer lands, which the form tracks as the rows touched since the pick began. What did not change: a site's silence is not an answer, switching sites falls back to carry-over and never to the site left behind (M2s), opening a stored dive never refills it, and nothing stored is migrated — a dive logged in the ocean with *fresh* before this keeps it until someone edits it.
